B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

疱疹病毒 媒介 中的 UL45–46 插入位点增强鸡对 H9N2 流感的保护

UL45–46 Insertion Site in Herpesvirus Vector Enhances Chicken Protection Against H9N2 Influenza
新闻简报2026年11月26日发表的一项研究,探索了利用火鸡疱疹病毒(HVT)表达H9N2 禽流感病毒血凝素抗原的重组疫苗。疫苗 靶向 HVT 基因组 的 UL45-46 基因间区域,与传统 US2 基因座相比,显示出卓越的免疫原性和保护功效。在无特定病原体鸡的对照试验中,HVT-HAUL45-46 构建体引发了显着更高的血凝抑制抗体滴度和更强的抗原特异性细胞 免疫应答。在用 H9N2 进行实验攻击后,接种疫苗的鸡表现出完全防止了泄殖腔病毒脱落、减少了口咽排泄并保留了肺组织。H9N2 亚型虽然致病性低,但会给家禽生产造成重大经济损失,并使鸡群容易发生继发感染。该研究强调了针对 UL45-46 区域增强 疫苗 有效性的潜力,尽管这些发现基于实验数据,尚未应用于现场设置。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于2026年11月26日。实验试验是在胚胎孵化第18.5天接种疫苗的鸡身上进行的,并在疫苗接种后28天观察结果。
地点与对象
该研究涉及无特定病原体的鸡进行对照试验,以 H9N2 禽流感 病毒为目标病原体。
事件
测试了使用火鸡疱疹病毒(HVT)表达 H9N2 禽流感 病毒血凝素抗原的重组 疫苗。疫苗 靶向 HVT 基因组 的 UL45-46 基因间区域,与传统 US2 基因座相比,显示出卓越的免疫原性和保护功效。
规模、影响与风险
H9N2亚型被归类为低致病性禽流感病毒,但它会导致商业家禽养殖中产蛋量、生长速度和饲料转化效率显着下降。它还使鸡群容易遭受严重的继发感染,从而导致 死亡率 急剧上升。研究表明,UL45-46 工程化的 媒介 完全阻止了泄殖腔病毒脱落并减少了口咽排泄,同时保留了肺组织结构。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是通过对照试验和分子分析进行的。结果通过侧翼引物 PCR、蛋白质印迹和间接免疫荧光测定进行了验证。这些发现基于实验数据,尚未应用于现场设置。该研究是开发针对 H9N2 更有效的 疫苗 的更广泛努力的一部分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

UL45–46 Insertion Site in Herpesvirus Vector Enhances Chicken Protection Against H9N2 Influenza

News Brief
A study published on November 26, 2026, explored a recombinant vaccine using the herpesvirus of turkeys (HVT) to express the hemagglutinin antigen of H9N2 avian influenza virus. The vaccine was targeted to the UL45–46 intergenic region of the HVT genome, showing superior immunogenicity and protective efficacy compared to the conventional US2 locus. In controlled trials with specific-pathogen-free chickens, the HVT-HAUL45–46 construct triggered significantly higher hemagglutination inhibition antibody titers and a stronger antigen-specific cellular immune response. Following an experimental challenge with H9N2, vaccinated chickens exhibited complete prevention of cloacal viral shedding, reduced oropharyngeal excretion, and preserved pulmonary tissue. The H9N2 subtype, while low pathogenicity, causes significant economic losses in poultry production and predisposes flocks to secondary infections. The study highlights the potential of targeting the UL45–46 region to enhance vaccine effectiveness, though these findings are based on experimental data and not yet applied in field settings.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health UL45–46 Insertion Site in Herpesvirus Vector Enhances Chicken Protection Against H9N2 Influenza by November 26, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 UL45–46 Insertion Site in Herpesvirus Vector Enhances Chicken Protection Against H9N2 Influenza Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A recombinant vaccine using the herpesvirus of turkeys to express the hemagglutinin antigen of H9N2 avian influenza virus demonstrated superior immunogenicity and protective efficacy when targeted to the viral genome’s UL45–46 intergenic region rather than the conventional US2 locus. In controlled trials involving specific-pathogen-free chickens, in-ovo administration of the construct designated HVT-HAUL45–46 triggered significantly higher hemagglutination inhibition antibody titers and a substantially stronger antigen-specific cellular immune response than the comparative US2-vectored candidate. Following an experimental respiratory and ocular challenge with pathogenic H9N2 virus, chickens immunized with the UL45–46-engineered vector exhibited complete prevention of cloacal viral shedding, reduced oropharyngeal excretion, decreased lung viral loads, and preserved pulmonary tissue architecture. The H9N2 subtype of avian influenza virus has transitioned over several decades from a localized pathogen into an endemic operational hazard across poultry production systems in Asia, the Middle East, North Africa, and sectors of Europe. While classified as a low-pathogenicity avian influenza virus producing relatively mild clinical manifestations under isolated conditions, field infections within commercial poultry operations trigger significant declines in egg production, retard growth rates in broiler chickens, and impair feed conversion efficiency. Furthermore, H9N2 infection damages respiratory mucosal barriers and induces substantial immunosuppression, predisposing domestic flocks to severe secondary bacterial and viral coinfections with agents such as Escherichia coli and infectious bronchitis virus, which drive acute spikes in flock mortality. Beyond production agriculture, H9N2 viruses present persistent public health and pandemic threats due to their established role as internal gene donors during viral reassortment events. Multiple emergent avian strains capable of infecting mammalian hosts, including H7N9, H10N8, and H3N8, acquired their internal gene cassettes from circulating H9N2 lineages. Epidemiological monitoring reported by international health authorities documented sporadic human spillover infections involving H9N2 between 2024 and early 2025 across regions such as China and Bangladesh, with a pronounced frequency among pediatric cases. Molecular analyses trace these zoonotic spillover events to adaptive mutations in the viral hemagglutinin glycoprotein, underscoring the necessity of controlling H9N2 transmission directly at the poultry reservoir level. Conventional poultry vaccination programs predominantly utilize whole-virus inactivated oil-emulsion formulations, which reduce mortality and clinical symptoms but exhibit clear limitations in halting transmission cycles. Because H9N2 is a mucosal pathogen colonizing respiratory and intestinal tracts, parenteral delivery of inactivated vaccines fails to induce robust mucosal secretory immunoglobulin A responses or sustained cell-mediated immunity. Consequently, birds receiving standard inactivated vaccines continue to shed infectious virus into farm environments. Recombinant herpesvirus of turkeys (HVT) vectors represent an alternative strategy due to their established safety profile, ability to induce life-long persistent infection with continuous endogenous antigen presentation, and simultaneous activation of systemic and cell-mediated immune pathways. The double-stranded DNA genome of HVT contains multiple nonessential insertion regions within its unique long (UL) and unique short (US) domains, but the functional selection of an integration site fundamentally influences vector replication and foreign antigen expression. The US2 region has served as a standard integration site, having previously demonstrated higher antibody responses than the US10 locus. However, genetic alterations within the US domain can negatively affect viral fitness and T-cell stimulation. Conversely, the UL45–46 intergenic region has shown clinical utility in commercial Newcastle disease vectors. Molecular insights from related alphaherpesviruses indicate that the tegument protein UL46 acts as an innate immune antagonist by binding TANK-binding kinase 1 and preventing its dimerization, thereby suppressing the TBK1–IRF3 signaling axis and downstream type I interferon production. Targeted disruption or cassette placement at UL45–46 may relieve this innate immune inhibition, enhancing vector adjuvanticity and antigen processing. To directly assess comparative performance, researchers rescued the HVT-HAUL45–46 viral construct by applying CRISPR/Cas9 endonuclease cleavage to the parental HVT-FC126 genome at the UL45–46 locus, inserting an expression cassette encoding the hemagglutinin gene derived from the H9N2 strain A/chicken/Anhui/AH463/2017 through nonhomologous end joining. Flanking-primer polymerase chain reaction validated site-specific integration by distinguishing the extended recombinant amplicon from the parental sequence. Western blot analysis verified stable hemagglutinin expression at the expected molecular weight of approximately 70 kilodaltons, while indirect immunofluorescence assays confirmed cytoplasmic localization within infected primary chicken embryo fibroblasts. Serial in vitro propagation across 20 consecutive passages confirmed that the inserted gene cassette remained genetically stable without detectable deletions or sequence mutations. Multistep in vitro growth kinetics measured in chicken embryo fibroblasts over 120 hours revealed that the insertion of the foreign antigen cassette did not compromise vector replication viability. Although parental HVT viral titers exhibited slightly higher replication metrics at 72 and 120 hours post-infection, the overall growth curve of HVT-HAUL45–46 closely mirrored the parental virus. These data established that genetic insertion into the UL45–46 intergenic boundary preserves fundamental viral replication kinetics while stably retaining the heterologous avian influenza protective antigen across successive viral generations. In vivo comparative immunogenicity was evaluated in specific-pathogen-free chicken embryos vaccinated at embryonic incubation day 18.5 via amniotic cavity injection with 5000 plaque-forming units of either HVT-HAUL45–46, HVT-HAUS2, or phosphate-buffered saline control. Systemic humoral monitoring demonstrated that hemagglutination inhibition antibodies appeared by 7 days post-immunization and progressively increased through day 28 in both vaccinated cohorts. However, by 28 days post-vaccination, the HVT-HAUL45–46 cohort attained an average hemagglutination inhibition titer of 3.58 ± 0.54 log2, significantly surpassing the level elicited by the HVT-HAUS2 construct. While serum neutralizing antibody titers peaked early at day 7 and gradually declined without significant differences between the two vaccinated groups, both vector candidates stimulated local neutralizing antibody production in harderian gland homogenates, tracheal washes, and duodenal washes. Antigen-specific cell-mediated immunity was quantified at 21 days post-immunization by harvesting splenocytes and performing ex vivo interferon-gamma enzyme-linked immunosorbent spot (ELISpot) assays following stimulation with inactivated H9N2 virus. Splenocytes isolated from birds receiving the HVT-HAUL45–46 vaccine generated an average of 675 ± 105 spot-forming cells per million splenocytes. This cellular response was significantly greater than the 243 ± 26 spot-forming cells per million recorded in the HVT-HAUS2 cohort, representing an approximate 2.8-fold elevation in interferon-gamma-secreting T lymphocytes. Unvaccinated control birds and sham-stimulated cultures exhibited virtually undetectable baseline responses, confirming the pronounced cellular stimulatory capacity associated with the UL45–46 locus. Protective viral clearance was verified by challenging vaccinated and control birds at 28 days post-immunization with 10^4 50% embryo infectious doses of H9N2 virus via combined intranasal and intraocular routes. In cloacal swab assessments performed at 3 and 5 days post-challenge, the HVT-HAUL45–46 vaccine completely prevented intestinal shedding, with zero out of ten birds shedding detectable virus at either time point. In contrast, the HVT-HAUS2 cohort exhibited cloacal shedding rates of 20 percent at day 3 and 40 percent at day 5, while the unvaccinated control group demonstrated 100 percent shedding. Oropharyngeal shedding was likewise attenuated; positive mucosal swabs in the HVT-HAUL45–46 group fell from 70 percent at day 3 to 60 percent at day 5, whereas the HVT-HAUS2 group recorded 90 percent and 80 percent shedding, and controls reached 100 percent positivity. Post-challenge lung examinations conducted at 5 days post-challenge demonstrated that both recombinant vaccines significantly lowered pulmonary viral loads relative to unvaccinated control birds. While quantitative reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction indicated lower mean viral titers in the HVT-HAUL45–46 cohort compared to HVT-HAUS2, this specific inter-vaccine titer difference did not achieve statistical significance. Histopathological evaluation via hematoxylin and eosin staining, however, revealed striking structural differences. Unvaccinated controls exhibited severe alveolar hemorrhage, vascular congestion, and marked inflammatory infiltration. Lungs from HVT-HAUS2-vaccinated chickens displayed moderate inflammatory lesions and pulmonary hemorrhage, whereas lung tissues from the HVT-HAUL45–46 cohort exhibited well-preserved tissue architecture with minimal inflammatory changes and substantially reduced vascular lesions. The collective experimental findings establish the UL45–46 intergenic junction as an advantageous insertion site for engineering herpesvirus-of-turkeys-vectored avian influenza vaccines. By sustaining high vector replication stability, generating elevated hemagglutination inhibition titers, eliciting potent splenic interferon-gamma cellular responses, and fully halting intestinal viral excretion, the HVT-HAUL45–46 construct addresses critical performance deficiencies inherent to conventional inactivated formulations and earlier recombinant designs. This platform presents an effective framework for advancing next-generation mucosal and cellular vaccines capable of interrupting H9N2 circulation within industrial poultry reservoirs. Time
The study was published on November 26, 2026. The experimental trials were conducted with chickens vaccinated at embryonic incubation day 18.5, and the results were observed up to 28 days post-vaccination.
Location and population
The research involved specific-pathogen-free chickens in controlled trials, with the H9N2 avian influenza virus as the target pathogen.
Event
A recombinant vaccine using the herpesvirus of turkeys (HVT) to express the hemagglutinin antigen of H9N2 avian influenza virus was tested. The vaccine was targeted to the UL45–46 intergenic region of the HVT genome, showing superior immunogenicity and protective efficacy compared to the conventional US2 locus.
Scale, impact and risk
The H9N2 subtype is classified as a low-pathogenicity avian influenza virus, but it causes significant declines in egg production, growth rates, and feed conversion efficiency in commercial poultry operations. It also predisposes flocks to severe secondary infections, which can lead to acute spikes in mortality. The study demonstrated that the UL45–46-engineered vector completely prevented cloacal viral shedding and reduced oropharyngeal excretion, with preserved pulmonary tissue architecture.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted using controlled trials and molecular analyses. The results were validated through flanking-primer PCR, Western blot, and indirect immunofluorescence assays. The findings are based on experimental data and are not yet applied in field settings. The research is part of a broader effort to develop more effective vaccines against H9N2.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

法国报告新 H5N1 暴发 禽流感重返波兰火鸡农场 - 自然世界新闻

Bird Flu Returns to Poland’s Turkey Farms as France Reports a New H5N1 Outbreak - Nature World News
新闻简报据路透社和 TVN24 援引世界动物卫生组织 (WOAH) 的报道,波兰瓦尔米亚-马祖里地区的一个火鸡养殖场和法国北部的一个家禽养殖场已确认感染高致病性 H5N1 禽流感 暴发。波兰 暴发 是六个月来的第一次,影响了 18,723 只鸟,其中 587 只死亡,其余被扑杀。在法国,4,300 只鸟中有 80 只死亡或被扑杀。暴发 与秋季鸟类迁徙有关,这增加了 传播 从 野生鸟类 传染给家禽的风险。在这两种情况下都没有人类感染的报道。当局已采取措施控制病毒,包括扑杀鸡群、清洁设施以及敦促家禽饲养者维持生物安全。波兰当地官员建议居民将家禽留在室内。法国 暴发 促使该国将禽流感风险级别提高至“高”,并对家禽实施严格的室内饲养规则。这些事件强调了 H5N1 暴发 的季节性特征及其对农业和保护的潜在影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 11 月 23 日得到确认,波兰 暴发 于上周检测到,法国 暴发 于上周四检测到。该文章发表于2026年11月24日。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在波兰的瓦尔米亚-马祖里地区,特别是利平基的一个火鸡农场,以及法国北部加莱海峡地区的一个家禽农场。受影响的种群包括火鸡、鸡和其他家禽品种。
事件
据世界动物卫生组织(WOAH)报道,路透社和 TVN24 援引,波兰的一个火鸡场和法国北部的一个家禽场确认出现高致病性 H5N1 禽流感 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
在波兰,18,723 只家禽中有 587 只死亡或被扑杀,该农场是该国最大的家禽出口地区的一部分。在法国,4,300 只鸟中有 80 只死亡或被扑杀。暴发 强调了病毒的季节性传播以及对家禽和 野生鸟类 的风险,但没有报告人类感染的情况。
应对、状态与不确定性
波兰和法国当局已实施根除该疾病的程序,包括扑杀鸡群、清洁和消毒场所以及设立控制区。当地官员敦促居民将家禽饲养在室内。法国暴发的详细信息基于路透社的报道,波兰的报道没有具体说明控制区域。目前情况正在调查和监测中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu Returns to Poland’s Turkey Farms as France Reports a New H5N1 Outbreak - Nature World News

News Brief
Highly pathogenic H5N1 bird flu outbreaks have been confirmed at a turkey farm in Poland's Warmian-Masurian region and a poultry farm in northern France, according to reports by the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) and cited by Reuters and TVN24. The Polish outbreak, the first in six months, affected 18,723 birds, with 587 deaths and the rest culled. In France, 80 out of 4,300 birds died or were culled. The outbreaks are linked to autumn bird migration, which increases the risk of transmission from wild birds to poultry. No human infections have been reported in either case. Authorities have taken measures to contain the virus, including culling flocks, cleaning facilities, and urging poultry keepers to maintain biosecurity. Local officials in Poland have advised residents to keep domestic birds indoors. The French outbreak prompted the country to raise its bird flu risk level to 'high,' implementing strict indoor housing rules for poultry. These events underscore the seasonal nature of H5N1 outbreaks and their potential impact on agriculture and conservation.
Original source text (English)
Home Animals Biology Environment Health & Medicine Tech Travel Space Natureworldnews.com Let's Connect Trending climate change Earth whales Bird Flu Returns to Poland’s Turkey Farms as France Reports a New H5N1 Outbreak By Elias Reed Published: Nov 24 2026, 5:39 AM EST Facebook Twitter Linkedin Comment Mail Alex Rusin From PEXELS Highly pathogenic H5N1 bird flu has struck a commercial turkey farm in northeastern Poland, the first such outbreak in the country in about six months. France has also reported a new farm outbreak in its north, as Europe enters the time of year when the virus usually spreads fastest. The World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH), citing reports from national authorities, confirmed both outbreaks on Monday, 23 November 2026. Neither outbreak has been linked to any human infection. But together they show how quickly the virus can reach farms once autumn bird migration is under way, and why governments are tightening rules on keeping poultry indoors. The Polish outbreak The Polish case was found on a farm in Lipinki, in the Biskupiec commune of the Warmian-Masurian region, according to Polish broadcaster TVN24. The farm raised more than 18,700 turkeys for slaughter. The provincial veterinary inspectorate in Olsztyn received laboratory results on Monday. Samples were tested at Poland's National Veterinary Research Institute in Puławy, which detected genetic material of the H5N1 avian influenza virus. According to Reuters, WOAH said 587 of the farm's 18,723 birds died, and the rest were culled. The inspectorate said procedures to eradicate the disease had been put in place. Local officials in Biskupiec urged residents to keep poultry and other domestic birds indoors and to follow the veterinary service's guidance. WOAH said this was Poland's first outbreak of highly pathogenic H5N1 in six months. That matters well beyond one farm. Poland is the European Union's largest poultry exporter, so outbreaks there can trigger trade restrictions and affect supply chains across the region. France confirms a northern case In France, the virus hit a poultry farm in the Pas-de-Calais area in the north of the country, Reuters reported, again citing WOAH. The disease killed 80 of the farm's 4,300 birds, and the remaining birds were culled. The report did not say what species the farm kept. The outbreak was detected the previous Thursday. A day later, France raised its national bird flu risk level to "high," the top tier, which brings rules such as keeping poultry confined indoors. TVN24 also noted France's move to its highest risk level. The details of the French outbreak in this article rest on the Reuters report of WOAH's notification. French authorities' own notice was not reviewed. Why autumn brings more outbreaks Bird flu outbreaks in Europe follow a strong seasonal pattern. As autumn arrives, millions of ducks, geese and swans migrate south and west from breeding grounds in northern Europe and Siberia. Wild waterbirds can carry avian influenza viruses, sometimes without showing signs of illness, and spread them along their routes. Farms become infected when the virus passes from wild birds into poultry houses. That can happen through direct contact with birds outdoors, through droppings on feed or water, or on boots, vehicles and equipment that move between areas. Once inside a large flock, a highly pathogenic strain can spread fast. Turkeys and chickens are especially vulnerable and often die in large numbers. This is why the standard response is so severe. When a highly pathogenic strain is confirmed, authorities usually cull the whole flock, clean and disinfect the site, and set up control zones around the farm with restrictions on moving birds and products. The Polish report did not give the size of any zones around the Lipinki farm. What it means for wild birds H5N1 is not only a farm problem. Since the early 2020s, the strain circulating widely around the world has killed large numbers of wild birds, including seabirds, cranes and birds of prey, and has spread to some mammals. Scientists have described it as a serious conservation threat as well as an agricultural one. The relationship works in both directions. Wild birds can bring the virus to farms, and large outbreaks among poultry can add to the amount of virus in the environment. This is one reason veterinary agencies ask people to report dead wild birds rather than handle them, and why many countries order poultry to be housed indoors during high-risk periods. Indoor housing reduces contact between domestic flocks and wild birds and their droppings. For readers, the advice is simple. People who find dead or sick wild birds, especially waterbirds or birds of prey, should not touch them and should report them to local veterinary or wildlife authorities. What farmers and bird keepers can do Biosecurity, the set of everyday habits that stop disease getting onto a farm, is the main defence. For commercial farms, that means keeping wild birds out of poultry houses, covering feed and water, changing boots and clothing at the door, cleaning vehicles and limiting visitors. Many outbreaks are traced back to small lapses, such as a gap in a roof or shared equipment moved between sites without cleaning. Backyard keepers matter too. Small flocks that roam outdoors near ponds or wetlands can come into contact with wild ducks and geese. That is why local officials in Biskupiec urged residents to keep domestic birds indoors. Owners who notice sudden deaths, drops in egg laying, or birds that are sluggish or have swollen heads should contact a vet or the local veterinary inspectorate rather than waiting. Some countries have added vaccination to their tools. France, for example, began vaccinating domestic ducks against bird flu in 2023, a step aimed at reducing outbreaks on duck farms. Vaccination can lower the number of infected flocks, but it does not replace biosecurity, and it can complicate trade because importing countries may want proof that vaccinated flocks are free of the virus. The Reuters report on the new French outbreak did not say whether vaccination was relevant to that farm. The cost of each outbreak For farmers, the loss of a flock is a severe blow. A whole house of birds can be gone within days, and restocking usually has to wait until cleaning and disinfection are complete and control zones are lifted. Governments in the EU often compensate farmers for culled birds, but income from lost production and disrupted contracts can still be large. For consumers, the effects show up more slowly, through prices and supply when outbreaks become widespread. Reuters noted that bird flu has disrupted supply and raised food prices in recent years. Is there a risk to people? Neither the Reuters reports nor TVN24 mentioned any human infection linked to these outbreaks. Reuters noted, in general terms, that bird flu has posed a risk of human transmission in recent years, but that was background rather than a finding about these farms. Human cases of H5N1 are rare and have usually involved close, unprotected contact with infected birds or animals. People who work with poultry are generally advised to use protective equipment during culls and clean-up. Properly cooked poultry and eggs are not considered a source of infection. What comes next Veterinary authorities in both countries will be watching for further cases in nearby farms. A first outbreak after a quiet spell, as in Poland, can be followed by others as wild bird migration continues and temperatures drop, conditions in which the virus survives longer in the environment. The coming weeks will show whether these are isolated events or the start of a busy season. Either way, the quick confirmation of both outbreaks through WOAH's reporting system gives farmers and officials across Europe early warning to tighten biosecurity before the virus spreads further. Related Routine Milk Testing Catches Oregon's First H5N1 Dairy Case While Union Says Sick Yakima Workers Are Being Missed Second Bird Reported With H5N1 at Nicosia's Athalassa Park as Cyprus Expert Calls Human Risk Very Limited Mutations From Canada's First Human H5N1 Case Weakened the Virus's Grip on Cells, UBC Study Finds H5N1 Die-Offs Prompt New Questions About Wildlife Extinction Risk Key Questions Answered Where was bird flu found in Poland? On a commercial turkey farm in Lipinki, in the Biskupiec commune of the Warmian-Masurian region in northeastern Poland. About 18,700 turkeys were on the farm. How many birds died or were culled? WOAH said 587 turkeys died and the rest of the 18,723 birds were culled. What happened in France? A poultry farm in Pas-de-Calais lost 80 of 4,300 birds to H5N1, and the rest were culled. France then raised its bird flu risk level to "high." Why does bird flu increase in autumn? Migrating wild waterbirds can carry the virus and spread it along their routes. Cold weather also helps the virus survive in the environment. Is there a risk to people? No human infections were reported in connection with these outbreaks. Human H5N1 cases are rare and usually involve close contact with infected birds. Tags Bird flu outbreak , Health © 2026 NatureWorldNews.com All rights reserved. Do not reproduce without permission. View five news elements
Time
The outbreaks were confirmed on 23 November 2026, with the Polish outbreak detected in the previous week and the French outbreak detected on the previous Thursday. The article was published on 24 November 2026.
Location and population
The outbreaks occurred in Poland's Warmian-Masurian region, specifically at a turkey farm in Lipinki, and in northern France, at a poultry farm in the Pas-de-Calais area. The affected population includes turkeys, chickens, and other poultry species.
Event
Highly pathogenic H5N1 bird flu outbreaks were confirmed at a turkey farm in Poland and a poultry farm in northern France, as reported by the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) and cited by Reuters and TVN24.
Scale, impact and risk
In Poland, 587 out of 18,723 birds died or were culled, and the farm was part of the country's largest poultry export region. In France, 80 out of 4,300 birds died or were culled. The outbreaks highlight the seasonal spread of the virus and the risk to poultry and wild birds, though no human infections were reported.
Response, status and uncertainty
Authorities in Poland and France have implemented procedures to eradicate the disease, including culling flocks, cleaning and disinfecting sites, and setting up control zones. Local officials urged residents to keep domestic birds indoors. The French outbreak's details are based on Reuters reports, and the Polish report did not specify control zones. The situation is under investigation and monitoring.

墨西哥动物园15名工作人员接触H5N1禽流感后出现症状

15 workers develop symptoms after H5N1 bird flu exposure at zoo in Mexico
新闻简报据当地卫生官员称,墨西哥瓜纳华托莱昂动物园的 15 名工作人员在接触 H5N1 禽流感后出现了症状。暴发被追踪到是野生候鸟,为了预防,动物园暂时关闭。州卫生当局确认了 29 名接触过病毒的工人,前五次检测结果呈阴性。预计未来几天会有更多结果。该病毒还导致两种哺乳动物(蜘蛛猴和虎猴)死亡,并感染了加拿大鹅、野鸭、秃鹰和鸵鸟等其他鸟类。动物园园长引用联邦和州卫生当局的建议宣布关闭。目前情况仍在调查中,尚未确定确定的风险级别。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于 2026 年 11 月 25 日报道,动物园于 2026 年 11 月 24 日星期三关闭。对暴露的工人进行了前五次测试,并于同一天得出阴性结果,预计未来几天将有更多结果。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在墨西哥瓜纳华托州的莱昂动物园。共有 29 名工人受到感染,其中 15 人出现症状。该病毒还感染了两种哺乳动物:蜘蛛猴和虎猴,以及其他鸟类,如加拿大鹅、野鸭、秃鹫和鸵鸟。
事件
当地卫生官员证实,墨西哥瓜纳华托莱昂动物园的 15 名工作人员在接触 H5N1 禽流感后出现了症状。暴发 被追踪到是野生候鸟,作为预防措施,动物园被关闭。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 导致 15 名出现症状的工人、29 名暴露者以及两只哺乳动物死亡。在多种鸟类中检测到了这种病毒,包括加拿大鹅、野鸭、秃鹫和鸵鸟。卫生当局尚未确认风险级别,但动物园的关闭表明这是一种预防措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
瓜纳华托州政府秘书豪尔赫·希门尼斯·洛纳 (Jorge Jimenez Lona) 表示,对接触过病毒的工人进行的前五次检测结果呈阴性,更多结果仍在等待中。动物园园长戴安娜·卡西利亚斯引用了联邦和州卫生当局关于关闭动物园的建议。暴发正在调查中,病毒来源追溯到野生候鸟。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

15 workers develop symptoms after H5N1 bird flu exposure at zoo in Mexico

News Brief
Fifteen workers at the Leon Zoo in Guanajuato, Mexico, developed symptoms after exposure to H5N1 bird flu, according to local health officials. The outbreak was traced to wild migratory birds, and the zoo was temporarily closed as a preventive measure. State health authorities identified 29 workers who were exposed, with the first five tests returning negative. More results are expected in the coming days. The virus also caused the death of two mammals, a spider monkey and a tigrillo, and infected other birds such as Canada geese, wild ducks, a vulture, and an ostrich. The closure was announced by the zoo director, citing recommendations from federal and state health authorities. The situation remains under investigation, with no confirmed risk level yet established.

美国流感季节:H5N1禽流感病例蔓延,7个州流感高峰,查看最新动态

Flu season in USA: H5N1 bird flu cases spread, high flu surge in 7 states, check latest updates
新闻简报The article reports on the early onset of the flu season in the USA, with high flu activity observed in seven states, including Washington, California, Louisiana, Oregon, South Carolina, Tennessee, and Texas. Three confirmed human cases of H5N1 bird flu were identified in Washington state, with one death, linked to individuals working on dairy farms in Yakima County where the virus was detected in cattle. The CDC noted that more than 3% of doctors’ office visits were for flu-like illness, signaling the flu season is underway. Genetic sequencing confirmed the infections were caused by the H5N1 virus, with strains matching those in dairy cattle. The WHO stated there is no evidence of human-to-human transmission. The CDC estimates at least 2.6 million illnesses, 36,000 hospitalizations, and 2,100 deaths so far this season are tied to flu infections. Some individuals have not received mandatory vaccine reminders, highlighting potential gaps in public health communication. The situation remains under investigation, with no evidence of widespread transmission.
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国的流感季节比预期提前开始,上周七个州报告流感活动较多。华盛顿州报告了三例人感染 H5N1 禽流感病例,其中 1 例死亡于 11 月 1 日。CDC 的最新更新发布于 2026 年 11 月 25 日。
地点与对象
暴发 主要报告于华盛顿州,特别是亚基马县,该县的牛中检测到了 禽流感 病毒。疾病预防控制中心指出,加利福尼亚州、路易斯安那州、俄勒冈州、南卡罗来纳州、田纳西州、德克萨斯州和华盛顿州也出现了高流感活动,影响了很大一部分人口。
事件
这些病例与在亚基马县奶牛场工作的个人有关,在那里的牛身上发现了病毒。疾病预防控制中心还报告称,七个州的流感活动活跃,表明流感病例出现更广泛的激增。
规模、影响与风险
The CDC estimates that at least 2.6 million illnesses, 36,000 hospitalizations, and 2,100 deaths so far this season are tied to flu infections. The H5N1 cases were confirmed through genetic sequencing, and there is no evidence of human-to-human transmission. However, the early-season flu activity is higher than usual, raising concerns about the potential impact on public health.
应对、状态与不确定性
疾病预防控制中心已发布最新更新,世界卫生组织(WHO)已收到有关病例的通知。没有证据表明 传播 存在人传人现象,CDC 正在监测情况。有些人没有收到有关流感 疫苗 的强制提醒,这表明公共卫生沟通中存在潜在差距。

While many Americans have fallen ill, some are yet to receive mandatory reminder message about vaccine shot. This comes as the World Health Organization said on Tuesday it had been notified by the United States of three confirmed human cases of bird ‌flu in Washington state, including one death. All three cases were adults who worked on dairy farms in Yakima County, Washington state, where the avian influenza virus was recently detected in cattle, the WHO said. WHO said there was no evidence so far of human-to-human transmission based on information available. The first patient developed mild symptoms, including eye irritation and a runny nose, and was not hospitalized. The second, who had underlying medical conditions, developed severe respiratory illness and died on November 1. The third developed conjunctivitis and recovered without hospitalization. Genetic sequencing confirmed all three infections were ‌caused by the H5N1 virus, with strains closely matching those detected in dairy cattle in Washington. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

南极海鸟栖息地禽流感谱系与南美洲有关 暴发

Antarctic Seabirds Harbor Avian Flu Lineages Linked to South American Outbreaks
新闻简报A study published on November 25, 2026, confirmed the active circulation of highly pathogenic avian influenza virus subtype H5N1 in seabird populations across the Antarctic Peninsula during the 2024–2025 austral summer. Field surveillance and genomic sequencing revealed that these Antarctic viral sequences share direct genetic connectivity with contemporary South American lineages, including strains detected in marine wildlife along the southern Atlantic coast. The investigation, conducted between November 2024 and January 2025, involved collecting 278 biological samples from four seabird species—gentoo penguins, chinstrap penguins, southern giant petrels, and south polar skuas—across the South Shetland Islands. Primary screening identified influenza A virus RNA in 30 specimens, with 25 out of 166 cloacal excreta samples testing positive (15.1 percent) and five of 56 cloacal swabs (8.9 percent). Whole-genome sequencing of two H5-positive isolates showed that the Antarctic sequences form a monophyletic cluster within South American clade 2.3.4.4b reference strains. The study highlights the potential for sustained viral dissemination across southern polar ecosystems and the risk of intercontinental ecological networks introducing emerging pathogens into dense Antarctic avifauna. The research was conducted in compliance with biosafety standards and environmental permits.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2024年至2025年南方夏季,调查于2024年11月至2025年1月进行。本研究旨在评估该时期患病率病毒的发生情况、患病率病毒和基因组结构。
地点与对象
南极半岛地区,特别是南设得兰群岛,包括乔治王岛、半月岛、罗伯特岛、利文斯顿岛、雪岛和尼尔森岛。受影响的种群包括常住海鸟和分布广泛的海鸟物种,如巴布亚企鹅、帽带企鹅、南方巨海燕和南极贼鸥。
事件
监测 现场证实了 高致病性禽流感 病毒 H5N1 亚型在南极洲常驻和迁徙 野生鸟类 人群中的活跃传播。该研究还建立了南极病毒序列与当代南美谱系之间的直接遗传联系,包括在南大西洋沿岸海洋野生动物中检测到的菌株。
规模、影响与风险
Primary screening confirmed the presence of influenza A virus matrix gene RNA in 30 out of 278 evaluated specimens, representing an overall diagnostic positivity of nearly eleven percent. Viral shedding was observed primarily through digestive pathways, with 25 out of 166 cloacal excreta samples testing positive (15.1 percent) and five of the 56 cloacal swabs (8.9 percent). The study highlights the potential for sustained viral dissemination across southern polar ecosystems and the risk of intercontinental ecological networks facilitating the introduction of emerging pathogens into dense Antarctic avifauna.
应对、状态与不确定性
这项调查是系统性的,涉及随后繁殖季节的分子监测。所有处理协议均符合机构和国际环境许可和生物安全标准。实验室处理在巴西南里奥格兰德联邦大学病毒学实验室进行,使用标准化方法,包括实时逆转录聚合酶链式反应和全 基因组 测序。该研究提供了病毒存在和基因连接性的经验证据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Antarctic Seabirds Harbor Avian Flu Lineages Linked to South American Outbreaks

News Brief
A study published on November 25, 2026, confirmed the active circulation of highly pathogenic avian influenza virus subtype H5N1 in seabird populations across the Antarctic Peninsula during the 2024–2025 austral summer. Field surveillance and genomic sequencing revealed that these Antarctic viral sequences share direct genetic connectivity with contemporary South American lineages, including strains detected in marine wildlife along the southern Atlantic coast. The investigation, conducted between November 2024 and January 2025, involved collecting 278 biological samples from four seabird species—gentoo penguins, chinstrap penguins, southern giant petrels, and south polar skuas—across the South Shetland Islands. Primary screening identified influenza A virus RNA in 30 specimens, with 25 out of 166 cloacal excreta samples testing positive (15.1 percent) and five of 56 cloacal swabs (8.9 percent). Whole-genome sequencing of two H5-positive isolates showed that the Antarctic sequences form a monophyletic cluster within South American clade 2.3.4.4b reference strains. The study highlights the potential for sustained viral dissemination across southern polar ecosystems and the risk of intercontinental ecological networks introducing emerging pathogens into dense Antarctic avifauna. The research was conducted in compliance with biosafety standards and environmental permits.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Antarctic Seabirds Harbor Avian Flu Lineages Linked to South American Outbreaks by November 25, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Antarctic Seabirds Harbor Avian Flu Lineages Linked to South American Outbreaks Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram During the 2024–2025 austral summer, field surveillance conducted across seabird breeding colonies in Antarctica confirmed the active circulation of highly pathogenic avian influenza virus subtype H5N1 within resident and migratory wild bird populations. Genomic sequencing and phylogenetic characterization of viral isolates obtained from the South Shetland Islands demonstrated that the circulating pathogens belong to the globally circulating clade 2.3.4.4b. Crucially, evolutionary analysis of complete viral gene segments established that these polar viral sequences share direct genetic connectivity with contemporary South American lineages, including strains detected among marine wildlife along the southern Atlantic coast. The investigation, carried out between November 2024 and January 2025, provides empirical evidence of sustained viral dissemination across southern polar ecosystems and illustrates how intercontinental ecological networks facilitate the introduction of emerging pathogens into dense Antarctic avifauna. The study was designed to evaluate the occurrence, prevalence, and genomic architecture of avian influenza viruses among both resident and wide-ranging seabird species that congregate in the Antarctic Peninsula region. Around one hundred million birds breed in the Antarctic Peninsula during the austral summer, assembling in dense colonies that present favorable conditions for viral transmission. While the Antarctic biome has historically experienced limited contact with foreign pathogens, resulting in native wildlife that generally lacks pre-existing immunological defenses, low-pathogenic avian influenza viruses have previously circulated at low rates of less than five percent. Earlier surveys recorded subtypes such as H11N2 in penguin excreta, followed in 2023 by the detection of subtypes H1N1 and H3N8 on King George Island. However, concerns regarding the introduction of highly pathogenic strains escalated dramatically following the October 2022 emergence of clade 2.3.4.4b in South America, where outbreaks along the Pacific coastline caused severe mortality across wild bird and marine mammal populations. Initial incursions into subantarctic territory were confirmed in late 2023 on Bird Island, South Georgia, followed shortly by detections on the Antarctic mainland, prompting systematic molecular monitoring during the subsequent breeding season. Field collections were carried out during the forty-third Antarctic Operation across diverse seabird nesting sites spanning the South Shetland Islands archipelago, including King George Island, Half Moon Island, Robert Island, Livingston Island, Snow Island, and Nelson Island. The investigative team assembled a total of 278 biological samples collected from four target species: gentoo penguins, chinstrap penguins, southern giant petrels, and south polar skuas. The specimen inventory comprised 166 non-invasive cloacal excreta samples, accompanied by paired sampling from 56 individual birds from which both cloacal swabs and orotracheal swabs were harvested, yielding 56 cloacal swabs and 56 orotracheal swabs. All handling protocols conformed strictly to institutional and international environmental permits alongside biosafety standards established by the Scientific Committee on Antarctic Research for managing avian influenza specimens in the Southern Ocean region. Laboratory processing was conducted at the Virology Laboratory within the Institute of Basic Health Sciences at the Federal University of Rio Grande do Sul in Brazil. Extraction of total ribonucleic acid was executed using the MagMAX CORE extraction kit, configured specifically for complex fecal and biological matrices. Approximately 0.3 grams of collected sample material was suspended in one milliliter of phosphate-buffered saline, homogenized by thorough vortexing, and clarified through centrifugation at 15,000 times gravity for one minute. Supernatant fractions measuring 200 microliters were processed on an automated KingFisher Flex platform, and purified ribonucleic acid was eluted into volumes of 50 microliters before storage at minus eighty degrees Celsius. Complementary deoxyribonucleic acid was subsequently generated using a commercial reverse transcription kit to prepare templates for amplification and molecular identification. Screening for influenza A virus was carried out via real-time reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction targeting a conserved 101-base-pair region of the viral matrix gene using TaqMan Fast Advanced Master Mix on a QuantStudio 1 real-time PCR platform. Thermal cycling parameters consisted of an initial incubation at 50 degrees Celsius for two minutes, denaturation at 95 degrees Celsius for twenty seconds, and forty cycles comprising 95 degrees Celsius for one second followed by annealing and extension at 60 degrees Celsius for twenty seconds with continuous fluorescence acquisition. Analytical sensitivity was established using a serial nine-point dilution series of a control plasmid containing the matrix gene spanning concentrations from 10 to the ninth power down to 10 to the first power copies per microliter. Quantitative results were evaluated using cycle threshold values, with readings below 30 categorized as representing high viral loads, measurements between 30 and 36 categorized as low positive detections, and values exceeding 36 deemed negative in the absence of a reproducible amplification curve. Primary screening confirmed the presence of influenza A virus matrix gene RNA in 30 out of the 278 evaluated specimens, representing an overall diagnostic positivity of nearly eleven percent across the study area. Viral shedding was observed primarily through digestive pathways, as 25 out of 166 cloacal excreta samples, or approximately 15.1 percent, tested positive, alongside five of the 56 cloacal swabs, or roughly 8.9 percent. In contrast, none of the 56 collected orotracheal swabs yielded positive amplifications, indicating that oral and respiratory shedding was negligible in this sample cohort. Positive specimens were detected in all four investigated avian species, encompassing resident divers such as gentoo penguins and chinstrap penguins, as well as highly dispersive flying seabirds including south polar skuas and southern giant petrels. Furthermore, viral presence was distributed across multiple geographic colonies within the archipelago, confirming that viral dispersal was regionally widespread across the South Shetland Islands rather than confined to a single localized breeding site. Following primary identification, all 30 matrix-positive specimens were screened with a secondary real-time reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction assay specific for the H5 hemagglutinin gene, targeting a 131-base-pair amplicon. Eight of the 30 matrix-positive samples, accounting for approximately 26 percent, were confirmed positive for the H5 subtype. To decipher the structural genetics and evolutionary trajectory of these viruses, whole-genome sequencing was executed on two H5-positive isolates using the Ion Torrent sequencing platform. Complete coding sequences for the surface glycoproteins hemagglutinin and neuraminidase were successfully generated from two distinct host species and geographical locations: one gentoo penguin sampled at Hennequin Point on King George Island and one southern giant petrel sampled at Two Summit on Nelson Island. Raw sequence data were processed, assembled, and refined using the Iterative Refinement Meta-Assembler influenza pipeline developed by the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention to reconstruct reliable consensus sequences. Genomic characterization of the reconstructed sequences confirmed that both Antarctic isolates represent highly pathogenic avian influenza virus subtype H5N1 within clade 2.3.4.4b. Amino acid profiling across the hemagglutinin protein identified a signature polybasic cleavage site motif containing the sequence arginine-glutamic acid-lysine-arginine-arginine-lysine-arginine-glycine-leucine-phenylalanine at the HA1-HA2 junction. The complete coding sequences were formally archived in the GenBank repository, with hemagglutinin sequences cataloged under accession numbers PX919726 for the gentoo penguin isolate and PX919694 for the southern giant petrel isolate, while corresponding neuraminidase sequences were deposited under accession numbers PX919746 and PX919737, respectively. Maximum-likelihood phylogenetic trees were constructed using MAFFT alignment, ModelFinder nucleotide substitution model selection, and IQ-TREE with one thousand ultrafast bootstrap iterations to ascertain evolutionary affiliations. Both the hemagglutinin and neuraminidase gene trees revealed that the Antarctic sequences form a robust, well-supported monophyletic cluster nested directly within contemporary South American clade 2.3.4.4b reference strains. Specifically, the Antarctic isolates clustered closely with viral strains identified in South American pinnipeds sampled in Uruguay during 2023, exhibiting node bootstrap support exceeding ninety percent. The congruent phylogenetic topologies observed between the hemagglutinin and neuraminidase segments demonstrate that both surface glycoproteins share an identical evolutionary history without evidence of recent genomic reassortment, indicating a pattern of clonal expansion and southward transmission from continental reservoirs. These genomic and molecular findings substantiate an epidemiological model wherein highly mobile, scavenging seabirds act as bridge hosts that transmit avian influenza viruses from South American coastal habitats across the Southern Ocean into the Antarctic ecosystem. Species such as south polar skuas, which undertake transequatorial migrations, and southern giant petrels, which regularly traverse marine corridors between the Antarctic Peninsula and southern South America, possess movement dynamics suited for introducing continental viral lineages into naive breeding colonies. Following introduction, high colony densities facilitate local transmission among resident species like gentoo and chinstrap penguins. India’s First Indigenous H5N1 Avian Influenza Vaccine - Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR)

News Brief
On November 24, 2026, the Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR) announced the development of India’s first indigenous H5N1 Avian Influenza vaccine by its National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD) in Bhopal. This breakthrough marks a significant advancement in the country’s agricultural science and disease control capabilities. The vaccine is designed to combat highly pathogenic avian influenza, which poses risks to poultry and can lead to zoonotic infections in humans. The development is confirmed as an official achievement, with no reported outbreak or specific case numbers mentioned. ICAR emphasizes the importance of such innovations in safeguarding both animal and human health, particularly in the context of avian-origin zoonotic infections. The vaccine’s creation is part of broader efforts to enhance agricultural resilience and public health preparedness.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

比哈尔邦那烂陀的死乌鸦中确认了 H5N1 禽流感 - 自然世界新闻

H5N1 Bird Flu Confirmed in Dead Crows in Nalanda, Bihar - Nature World News
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 22 日,印度比哈尔邦那烂陀县比哈尔沙里夫县治安官官邸发现四只乌鸦死亡,这些乌鸦被送往巴特那、加尔各答和博帕尔的实验室后,检测结果呈 H5N1 禽流感阳性。这些乌鸦于 11 月 6 日被发现,确认过程花了两周多的时间。当局在比哈尔沙里夫周围建立了一个 12 公里长的 监测 区域,以监测家禽养殖场并收集鸡肉样本进行检测。据官员称,该地区没有养鸡场确诊禽流感或报告鸡异常死亡。乌鸦可能是通过接触来自西伯利亚和其他寒冷地区的候鸟和鸭子而被感染的。虽然该病毒对鸟类具有高致病性,但官员尚未报告任何扑杀情况。他们建议人们不要徒手处理死亡或患病的鸟类,并报告任何异常的鸟类死亡。监测 旨在及早发现禽流感病毒的传播,因为乌鸦通常是禽流感传播的早期预警信号。应对措施包括对受影响区域进行消毒和组建快速反应小组。确认的延迟凸显了测试 野生鸟类 样本的挑战。目前尚未确认人类病例,但官员警告称,与受感染鸟类的密切接触可能给人们带来潜在风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于2026年11月22日报道,乌鸦于2026年11月6日被发现死亡。监测区域于2026年11月22日宣布,H5N1的确认在初步测试后花了两周多的时间。
地点与对象
该事件发生在印度比哈尔邦那烂陀区,特别是主要城镇比哈尔沙里夫。受影响人群包括比哈尔沙里夫 12 公里半径范围内的家禽养殖场和当地社区,其中乌鸦是主要的 宿主。
事件
博帕尔国家高安全动物疾病研究所证实,比哈尔沙里夫地区治安官官邸发现的四只乌鸦 H5N1 禽流感检测呈阳性。据当地媒体和 ETV Bharat 报道,这是一例乌鸦 H5N1 确诊病例。
规模、影响与风险
全区无养鸡场确诊禽流感或报告鸡异常死亡。当局设立了一个12公里长的监测区域,并正在采集鸡样本进行检测。人们注意到,通过与受感染的鸟类密切接触可能对人类造成风险,但尚未报告确诊的人类病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
快速反应小组已成立,全区兽医、畜牧工作人员均已待命。县长官邸及附近区域喷洒了消毒剂。监测 正在进行中,家禽养殖场的结果将决定进一步的行动。确认的延迟凸显了 野生鸟类 测试样本的复杂性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 Bird Flu Confirmed in Dead Crows in Nalanda, Bihar - Nature World News

News Brief
In Nalanda district, Bihar, India, four crows found dead at the district magistrate's residence in Biharsharif tested positive for H5N1 bird flu on 22 November 2026, after being sent through laboratories in Patna, Kolkata, and Bhopal. The crows were discovered on 6 November, and the confirmation process took over two weeks. Authorities have established a 12-kilometre surveillance zone around Biharsharif to monitor poultry farms and collect chicken samples for testing. No poultry farm in the district has confirmed bird flu or reported abnormal chicken deaths, as stated by officials. The crows may have been infected through contact with migratory birds and ducks arriving from Siberia and other cold regions. While the virus is highly pathogenic in birds, officials have not reported any culling yet. They have advised people not to handle dead or sick birds with bare hands and to report any unusual bird deaths. The surveillance aims to detect any spread to poultry early, as crows are often an early warning sign of bird flu circulation. The response includes disinfection of the affected area and the formation of a rapid response team. The delay in confirmation underscores the challenges in testing wild bird samples. No human cases have been confirmed, but officials warn of the potential risk to people through close contact with infected birds.
Original source text (English)
Home Animals Biology Environment Health & Medicine Tech Travel Space Natureworldnews.com Let's Connect Trending climate change Earth whales Bird Flu in Crows Triggers 12 km Alert Zone in Bihar By Elias Reed Published: Nov 23 2026, 9:54 AM EST Facebook Twitter Linkedin Comment Mail INDRANIL MUKHERJEE/AFP via Getty Images Four crows found dead at the official residence of the district magistrate in Biharsharif, the main town of Nalanda district in the Indian state of Bihar, have tested positive for the H5N1 strain of bird flu, local media reported on Sunday, 22 November 2026. The crows were found on 6 November inside the district magistrate's residence complex, Amar Ujala reported. Samples were sent to laboratories in Patna and Kolkata and finally to the National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases in Bhopal, India's top laboratory for dangerous animal diseases, which confirmed H5N1. Authorities have declared a 12-kilometre surveillance zone around Biharsharif, where chicken samples from poultry farms are being collected and tested. So far, no unusual deaths or bird flu cases have been found on any poultry farm in the district, officials said. What officials are doing According to Amar Ujala, ETV Bharat and First Bihar, the district administration has taken several steps. A rapid response team has been formed, and leave for all district veterinarians and animal husbandry staff has been cancelled with immediate effect so they are available for emergencies. With help from the municipal corporation, disinfectant was sprayed across the district magistrate's residence and the nearby DM colony. Poultry farms within 12 km are under surveillance, with chicken samples being collected for testing. Block-level teams are monitoring poultry farms and bird movements in rural areas, Amar Ujala said. A four-member expert team from the Animal Health and Production Institute in Patna visited Biharsharif on Saturday, 21 November, met the district animal husbandry officer, reviewed the situation and drew up a containment plan, ETV Bharat and First Bihar reported. District Animal Husbandry Officer Dr. Rekha Choubey said no poultry farm in the district had confirmed bird flu and no abnormal chicken deaths had been reported, ETV Bharat said. Officials described this as a relief. How did crows get bird flu? Officials suspect the virus arrived with migratory birds. Large numbers of migratory birds and ducks arrive at the district's reservoirs at this time of year, and officials said the crows may have been infected through contact with infected birds, First Bihar reported. Dr. Choubey said the crows may have caught the virus from migratory birds and ducks arriving from Siberia and other cold regions, according to Amar Ujala. This is a suspicion, not a confirmed finding. Crows are scavengers that feed on carcasses, rubbish and food scraps. That habit can expose them to the virus if they eat dead or sick birds. Because crows live close to people in towns and cities, deaths among them are often one of the first visible signs that bird flu is circulating in an area. Why crows are an early warning India has seen repeated outbreaks of H5N1 in crows. Earlier in 2026, between about 1,000 and 1,500 crows died across Chennai, and samples tested at the same Bhopal laboratory confirmed H5N1 on 5 February, prompting statewide surveillance in Tamil Nadu, The Logical Indian reported. Crow deaths were also linked to H5N1 in Kerala's Kannur district in January, Telangana Today reported. Bihar itself has been here before this year. In late January and early February, authorities in Darbhanga district confirmed H5N1 after a sudden spike in crow deaths, NewsBytes reported. India also reported 11 outbreaks of H5N1 on poultry farms in December 2025, according to the World Organisation for Animal Health, as cited by Reuters and Down To Earth. Officials across India describe wild bird deaths like these as early warning signals that the virus is circulating in the environment. Because crows are so common and visible, a cluster of dead crows is more likely to be noticed and reported than deaths among wild ducks on a distant lake. That is why the Nalanda samples were sent for testing in the first place, after the birds were found inside one of the most closely watched compounds in the district, even though it took more than two weeks for the result to be confirmed after passing through three laboratories. The delay highlights how long confirmation can take for samples from wild birds. What H5N1 means for poultry H5N1 is a highly pathogenic avian influenza virus, meaning it can kill large numbers of chickens very quickly. Culling is not possible for wild crows, which is why the response to crow deaths focuses on surveillance, disinfection and protecting nearby poultry, as Kerala officials noted after earlier crow cases, according to Onmanorama. If the virus reaches a poultry farm, standard practice in India is to cull birds within a set radius of the outbreak, disinfect the area and restrict the movement of poultry and eggs. That can cause serious losses for farmers. The current surveillance around Biharsharif is designed to catch any spread to poultry early. Officials have not reported any culling, because no farm infections have been found. Is there a risk to people? H5N1 mainly affects birds, but people can be infected through close contact with infected birds, especially sick or dead ones, or their droppings. Human cases worldwide have been rare, but some have been severe. Officials in Nalanda have advised people not to handle dead or sick birds with bare hands and to report any unusual bird deaths to the animal husbandry department immediately. Poultry workers, bird keepers and chicken and egg sellers have been asked to take extra precautions, Amar Ujala said. Anyone who has been in contact with birds and develops fever, cough or difficulty breathing should see a doctor promptly, ETV Bharat and First Bihar reported. Properly cooked chicken and eggs are not considered a source of infection, according to health authorities, as cooking kills the virus. What comes next Test results from poultry farms in the 12 km zone will show whether the virus has spread beyond wild birds. With the migratory season continuing into winter, officials in Nalanda and across Bihar are likely to stay on alert for further bird deaths. Given this year's earlier crow outbreaks in Darbhanga, Chennai and Kerala, the coming months could bring more detections elsewhere in India too. Related H5N1 Die-Offs Prompt New Questions About Wildlife Extinction Risk Oregon Confirms Its First Human Bird Flu Case of 2026, Linked to a Dairy Herd Bird Flu Investigation Begins After Significant Poultry Deaths in Northern Ireland Ibis Deaths at Bendigo's Lake Weeroona Climb to 79 as Victoria Works to Rule Out H5 Bird Flu Key Questions Answered What was found in Nalanda, Bihar? Four crows found dead on 6 November 2026 at the district magistrate's residence in Biharsharif tested positive for H5N1 bird flu, according to reports on 22 November. Which laboratory confirmed the bird flu? The National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases in Bhopal confirmed H5N1 after samples passed through labs in Patna and Kolkata. Has bird flu reached poultry farms in Nalanda? No. Officials said no poultry farm in the district had confirmed bird flu and no abnormal chicken deaths had been reported. Chicken samples from farms within 12 km of Biharsharif are being collected and tested. How did the crows get infected? Officials suspect migratory birds and ducks arriving at the district's reservoirs brought the virus, and that the crows were infected through contact with them, but this has not been confirmed. Can people catch H5N1 from crows? Human infections are rare and usually involve close contact with sick or dead birds. People should not touch dead birds with bare hands, should report them to the animal husbandry department, and should see a doctor if they develop fever, cough or breathing difficulty after contact with birds. WHO confirms three cases of H5N1 bird flu in the United States, with one fatality - Diari ARA

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) confirmed three cases of H5N1 bird flu in humans in the United States, with one fatality, as reported on 25 November 2026. All affected individuals worked on dairy farms in the state of Washington. The infections were confirmed by the WHO, and there is currently no evidence of human-to-human transmission. The situation remains under investigation, with no further details on the spread or risk level provided. The WHO has not issued additional prevention or treatment measures at this time. The report highlights the potential risk of avian-origin zoonotic infections, emphasizing the need for continued monitoring and containment efforts.

研究人员在中国家禽中发现了新型 禽流感 病毒 - SuaraGarut.ID

Researchers Identify Novel Avian Influenza Virus in Chinese Poultry - SuaraGarut.ID
新闻简报2025年12月,中国河南省商丘市爆发了一种严重的呼吸道疾病暴发,兽医研究人员因此鉴定出一种新型禽流感病毒,命名为A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025或NL04。该病毒属于低致病性H6N2亚型,但具有来自不同禽流感谱系的不寻常基因组合。它在一群54周龄的商品蛋鸡中被发现,导致严重的呼吸道症状和15 percent的死亡率率。血凝素基因来源于与水禽相关的H6谱系,而神经氨酸酶基因则来自与家禽相关的H9N2谱系。六个内部基因片段来自适应鸡类的H9N2 G57基因型。实验研究表明,尽管商业鸡群出现了显著的疾病和死亡,但实验室感染的鸡只却未出现临床症状而存活。该病毒保留了与低致病性禽流感一致的血凝素切割位点,但其遗传特性表明可能具有抗病毒药物抗性。这些发现强调了对禽流感病毒进行监测的重要性,以评估其进化潜力及其对家禽健康的影响。

暴发于2025年12月报道,研究结果于2026年11月23日发表。

兽医研究人员在发生严重呼吸道疾病 暴发 后,在中国河南省商丘市的商业家禽中发现了一种新型 禽流感 病毒,命名为 A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025 或 NL04。Bioengineer.org 发表的研究证实了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak resulted in a 15 percent cumulative mortality rate among the infected chickens. The virus carries genetic characteristics associated with antiviral resistance but retains a hemagglutinin cleavage site consistent with low-pathogenic avian influenza. No explicit risk level was stated, but the unusual genetic combination suggests potential for increased virulence.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Researchers Identify Novel Avian Influenza Virus in Chinese Poultry - SuaraGarut.ID

News Brief
In December 2025, a severe respiratory disease outbreak in Shangqiu, Henan Province, China, led to the identification of a novel avian influenza virus, designated A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025 or NL04, by veterinary researchers. The virus belongs to the low-pathogenic H6N2 subtype but has an unusual genetic combination from different avian influenza lineages. It was found to cause severe respiratory symptoms and a 15 percent mortality rate in a commercial layer flock of 54-week-old chickens. The hemagglutinin gene originated from a waterfowl-associated H6 lineage, while the neuraminidase gene came from the poultry-associated H9N2 lineage. The six internal gene segments were from the chicken-adapted H9N2 G57 genotype. Experimental studies showed that while the commercial flock experienced significant illness and death, laboratory-infected chickens survived without clinical symptoms. The virus retained a hemagglutinin cleavage site consistent with low-pathogenic avian influenza, but its genetic traits suggest potential for antiviral resistance. These findings highlight the importance of monitoring avian influenza viruses for their potential to evolve and impact poultry health.
Original source text (English)
Researchers Identify Novel Avian Influenza Virus in Chinese Poultry Menu Close Up-to-Date Information, Close to the Community. Home Business Lifestyle Tutorials Technology Politics Education Sports Public Info Automotive Opinion Economy Health Discover more Get Cold Relief Check Symptoms Study Biology Home Health Researchers Identify Novel Avian Influenza Virus in Chinese Poultry Researchers Identify Novel Avian Influenza Virus in Chinese Poultry Ashley Morgan Published Nov 24, 2026 - 02:48 4 Reads Last Update Nov 24, 2026 - 02:48 Share WhatsApp Outbreak Smallest Font Largest Font Veterinary researchers have identified a previously undocumented avian influenza virus in commercial poultry in Shangqiu, Henan Province, China, after investigating a severe respiratory disease outbreak in December 2025. The virus, designated A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025 or NL04, belongs to the low-pathogenic H6N2 subtype but carries an unusual genetic combination associated with multiple avian influenza lineages. According to research published by bioengineer.org on November 23, 2026, the outbreak affected a commercial layer flock consisting of 54-week-old chickens. The infection caused severe respiratory symptoms and a cumulative mortality rate of 15 percent. Infected birds experienced elevated body temperatures, extreme lethargy, reduced feed consumption, and breathing difficulties. Some chickens died within three to four days after symptoms appeared. Postmortem examinations revealed substantial airway obstruction caused by long, white, mucus-like plugs inside the bronchial passages. Researchers identified these obstructions as the primary cause of suffocation and death. The examinations also detected yolk sac peritonitis, with lesions more severe than those typically associated with low-pathogenic avian influenza. Whole-genome sequencing revealed that NL04 combines genetic material from different avian influenza lineages. Its hemagglutinin gene originated from a waterfowl-associated H6 lineage, while its neuraminidase gene came from the poultry-associated H9N2 lineage. The six internal gene segments, identified as PB2, PB1, PA, NP, M, and NS, originated from the chicken-adapted H9N2 G57 genotype. Their nucleotide sequence identities ranged from 96.38 percent to 99.12 percent. Researchers also identified several molecular characteristics associated with viral replication, host adaptation, and antiviral resistance. These included the S31N substitution in the matrix 2 protein, which is associated with resistance to adamantane antiviral drugs, including amantadine. Despite these genetic characteristics, the virus retained a hemagglutinin cleavage site consistent with low-pathogenic avian influenza. Researchers also found that the canonical mammalian receptor-binding mutations Q226L and G228S were absent. To investigate the virus's biological behavior, researchers conducted experiments involving two-week-old specific-pathogen-free chickens. The birds received an infectious dose of 10^6.0 EID50 through combined ocular and intranasal inoculation. Unlike the commercial flock in Henan, all experimentally infected chickens survived the 14-day observation period. They showed no obvious clinical symptoms, including fever, depression, lethargy, or diarrhea. Their weight gain also showed no statistically significant difference compared with uninfected control animals. However, microscopic examinations revealed significant tissue damage despite the absence of obvious clinical illness. Tracheal samples showed extensive loss of epithelial cilia, while lung examinations identified alveolar atrophy and inflammatory cell infiltration. Researchers also detected kidney abnormalities, including glomerular epithelial cell shrinkage and infiltration by red and white blood cells. Immunohistochemical testing confirmed viral nucleoprotein antigen in lung alveolar epithelial cells, providing evidence of viral replication in respiratory tissues. Viral measurements taken three days after infection showed the highest concentrations in the upper respiratory tract and associated ocular tissues. Nasal turbinate samples reached 10^5.56 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter, while the Harderian gland recorded 10^4.23 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter. Both tracheal and bronchial samples recorded viral concentrations of 10^1.82 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter. These findings demonstrated that NL04 primarily replicated in respiratory tissues under the experimental conditions. The contrasting outcomes between the commercial flock and laboratory-infected chickens highlight a substantial difference in disease severity under different production conditions. The commercial outbreak produced 15 percent mortality, whereas experimentally infected chickens survived the entire monitoring period without apparent clinical illness.
