B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

在中国家禽中发现新型 禽流感 重配病毒 暴发 - Bioengineer.org

Novel Avian Influenza Reassortant Virus Discovered in Chinese Poultry Outbreak - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报2025 年 12 月,在中国河南省商丘市的家禽 暴发 中鉴定出一种新型 禽流感 重配病毒 A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025。这种低致病性 H6N2 亚型结合了来自不同 禽流感 谱系的遗传片段,包括来自水禽起源的 H6 谱系的血凝素基因和来自 H9N2 家禽谱系的神经氨酸酶基因。暴发 导致受影响鸡群的 死亡率 发病率为 15%,出现严重的呼吸道症状和卵黄囊腹膜炎体征。全基因组测序显示,内部基因片段继承自H9N2 G57基因型,该基因型在陆生家禽中很常见。实验研究表明,接种该病毒的无特定病原体的鸡能够存活,没有明显的临床症状,但显微镜分析显示存在广泛的组织损伤。该研究强调了野生鸟类和家养鸟类之间遗传重配的可能性,引发了人们对该病毒的适应性以及在家禽种群中持续进行 基因组监测 的需求的担忧。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2025年12月报道,新闻文章发表于2026年11月23日。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在中国河南省商丘市,影响了一群 54 周龄的商业蛋鸡群。
事件
在中国中部的家禽 暴发 中鉴定并鉴定了一种新型重配 禽流感 病毒,命名为 A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025。该病毒属于低致病性 H6N2 亚型,但携带不寻常的基因组成,结合了来自不同 禽流感 谱系的片段。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 在受影响的鸡群中造成了 15% 的累积 死亡率 率。该病毒被证实为低致病性,缺乏高致病性菌株典型的连续碱性氨基酸。然而,它在神经氨酸酶和其他蛋白质中表现出毒力标记,可能会增强其对哺乳动物 宿主 的致病性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该调查涉及全 基因组 测序、尸检以及对无特定病原体鸡的实验接种。研究结果表明,该病毒并非通过遗传漂变获得内部片段,而是继承了完整的核糖核蛋白盒。然而,该研究存在局限性,包括缺乏体外受体结合测定和气雾剂 传播 实验。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Novel Avian Influenza Reassortant Virus Discovered in Chinese Poultry Outbreak - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A novel avian influenza reassortant virus, A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025, was identified in a poultry outbreak in Shangqiu, Henan Province, China, in December 2025. This low-pathogenic H6N2 subtype combines genetic segments from different avian influenza lineages, including the hemagglutinin gene from a waterfowl-origin H6 lineage and the neuraminidase gene from an H9N2 poultry lineage. The outbreak resulted in a 15% mortality rate in the affected flock, with severe respiratory symptoms and signs of yolk sac peritonitis. Whole-genome sequencing revealed that the internal gene segments were inherited from the H9N2 G57 genotype, which is common in terrestrial poultry. Experimental studies showed that specific-pathogen-free chickens inoculated with the virus survived without overt clinical symptoms, but microscopic analysis revealed extensive tissue damage. The study highlights the potential for genetic reassortment between wild and domestic birds, raising concerns about the virus's adaptability and the need for continued genomic surveillance in poultry populations.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 27, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Novel Avian Influenza Reassortant Virus Discovered in Chinese Poultry Outbreak by November 23, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 7 mins read 0 Novel Avian Influenza Reassortant Virus Discovered in Chinese Poultry Outbreak Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Veterinary researchers investigating an acute respiratory disease outbreak in commercial poultry in central China have identified and characterized a novel reassortant avian influenza virus designated A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025, or NL04. The natural reassortant belongs to the low-pathogenic H6N2 subtype but carries an unusual genetic composition that combines external and internal gene segments from divergent avian influenza lineages. The outbreak occurred in December 2025 within a fifty-four-week-old commercial layer flock located in Shangqiu, Henan Province, causing severe respiratory symptoms and a fifteen percent cumulative mortality rate. Affected birds exhibited severe clinical signs including elevated body temperature, profound lethargy, reduced feed intake, neck extension during respiration, and acute death within three to four days of onset. Postmortem examinations of affected field birds showed severe bronchial obstruction caused by long white exudative plugs occluding the airways, which investigators identified as the primary cause of suffocation and death. Additional necropsy findings in the field flock revealed signs of yolk sac peritonitis, presenting a lesion severity substantially greater than that typically documented during standard low-pathogenic avian influenza infections. Whole-genome sequencing of the isolated NL04 strain revealed an intricate reassortment profile that integrates segments from two major circulating avian influenza lineages. The hemagglutinin gene was traced to a waterfowl-origin H6 lineage showing 96.59 percent nucleotide identity to known sequences and clustering within Eurasian group I, also designated the ST339-like lineage. In contrast, the neuraminidase gene derived from an H9N2 poultry lineage, exhibiting 99.37 percent nucleotide homology with contemporary circulating H9N2 isolates and falling within the Eurasian Y280-like lineage. Crucially, all six internal gene segments—PB2, PB1, PA, NP, M, and NS—were inherited from the chicken-adapted H9N2 G57 genotype, exhibiting high nucleotide identities between 96.38 percent and 99.12 percent. While initial sequence alignment identified top basic local alignment matches for the PB2 and M genes in H3N3 and H3N8 isolates, phylogenetic analyses demonstrated that those matching segments were themselves derived from circulating H9N2 G57 viruses. This genomic constellation demonstrates that NL04 did not acquire its internal segments through piecemeal genetic drift, but rather inherited an intact ribonucleoprotein cassette and structural core that is widely established in terrestrial poultry. Analysis of molecular virulence markers in the primary sequences revealed critical structural attributes governing host range, drug susceptibility, and baseline pathogenicity. The hemagglutinin cleavage site of the NL04 isolate possessed the sequence PQIETR↓G, lacking the consecutive basic amino acids that characterize highly pathogenic avian influenza viruses and thereby confirming its classification as a low-pathogenic strain. The canonical mammalian receptor-binding mutations Q226L and G228S were absent in the hemagglutinin protein, with residues Q226 and G228 fully preserved. However, analysis of the neuraminidase protein identified a deletion within the stalk region spanning residues 62 to 64, a structural change that has previously been linked to elevated virulence in mammalian hosts, alongside a K368N substitution associated with immune escape and antigenic drift. Within the matrix 2 protein, the isolate harbored the S31N substitution, which confers pharmacological resistance to the adamantane class of antiviral drugs, specifically amantadine. Discover more Check Symptoms Get Cold Relief Study Biology Order DNA Kits Buy Fresh Poultry The six internal gene products contained numerous non-canonical substitutions previously documented to enhance polymerase activity and replication kinetics in distinct genetic backgrounds. The PB2 protein carried the specific substitutions A588V, T598I, and A676T, all associated with heightened viral replication and polymerase efficiency in experimental models, while lacking the canonical mammalian adaptive markers A199S, I292V, and E627K. In the PB1 protein, residues 473V and I368V were present, mutations linked respectively to enhanced polymerase performance in mammalian cell systems and respiratory droplet transmissibility in ferrets. The polymerase acidic subunit contained K356R, associated with enhanced mammalian replication, alongside the avian-mammalian species marker S409N. Further markers were detected in the nucleoprotein, where L3S was identified, and in the matrix 1 protein, which exhibited the species signature T215A and the virulence-associated change N30D. Finally, the non-structural protein NS1 carried four recognized substitutions—P42S, E172K, N205S, and P212S—known to modify virulence and interferon antagonism in mammalian experimental hosts. To rigorously determine the intrinsic biological behavior of the isolate without the confounding environmental variables of intensive farming, researchers inoculated two-week-old specific-pathogen-free chickens via combined ocular and intranasal routes using an infectious dose of 10^6.0 EID50. In stark contrast to the devastating fifteen percent mortality recorded in the commercial Henan flock, all inoculated specific-pathogen-free chickens survived the full fourteen-day experimental monitoring period. The infected birds displayed no overt clinical abnormalities, remaining free of fever, depression, lethargy, or diarrheal illness, and their body weight gain showed no statistically significant difference when compared with uninfected phosphate-buffered saline control animals. Gross pathological evaluation performed at three days post-infection showed only subtle organ changes, including focal hemorrhagic spots within the Harderian gland, mild enlargement and petechial hemorrhages in the thymus, and slight bursal swelling with minor mucoid exudation, while respiratory organs such as the trachea and lungs showed no macroscopic lesions. Despite the lack of overt clinical illness and visible macroscopic pathology in experimental chickens, microscopic histopathological evaluation revealed extensive underlying tissue damage throughout the respiratory tract. Examination of hematoxylin and eosin-stained tracheal sections demonstrated severe ciliary shedding along the epithelial lining without overt hemorrhagic extravasation. In lung sections, alveolar atrophy was prominent and accompanied by substantial infiltration of inflammatory cells into interstitial spaces, while kidney tissues showed glomerular epithelial karyopyknosis with erythrocyte and leukocyte infiltration. Immunohistochemical staining confirmed viral replication in the respiratory epithelium, revealing abundant viral nucleoprotein antigen in lung alveolar epithelial cells. Semi-quantitative histopathological scoring confirmed that tissue lesions were most severe in the lungs and trachea, followed by the kidneys. Quantitative viral titration of tissues at three days post-infection established that viral replication peaked in the upper respiratory tract and associated ocular tissues, reaching 10^5.56 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter in the nasal turbinates and 10^4.23 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter in the Harderian gland, while trachea and bronchus titers both reached 10^1.82 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter, confirming strict respiratory tropism. The marked discrepancy between mild experimental laboratory infections in specific-pathogen-free chickens and the severe mortality observed in the commercial Henan flock highlights the profound influence of secondary production factors. In industrial poultry operations, high-density stocking, environmental ammonia accumulation, ventilation stress, and concurrent microbial exposures create an environment highly permissive to exacerbated clinical disease. Primary viral replication by low-pathogenic strains such as NL04 damages the respiratory ciliated epithelium and compromises mucociliary clearance, rendering birds highly vulnerable to secondary opportunistic bacterial invaders such as Escherichia coli and Mycoplasma species. These secondary pathogens frequently induce severe exudative fibrinous airsacculitis and bronchial occlusion, explaining the fatal plugs observed in the commercial outbreak. The experimental findings emphasize that specific-pathogen-free animal trials alone cannot predict the true veterinary and economic impact of emerging avian influenza variants in real-world commercial poultry operations. To evaluate potential mammalian spillover risk, the research team intranasally challenged six-week-old female BALB/c mice with 10^6.00 EID50 of the purified NL04 virus. Over the fourteen-day evaluation period, all infected mice survived without displaying detectable respiratory distress, ruffled fur, or statistically significant weight loss relative to sham-inoculated controls. Organ titration at three days post-infection demonstrated limited replication, with viral titers confined predominantly to the upper respiratory tract at 10^3.25 EID50 per 0.1 milliliter in nasal turbinates, accompanied by minimal levels of 10^1.56 in lung tissue and 10^1.18 in kidney tissue. Brain samples showed no viral replication, and histological sections revealed only mild alveolar inflammatory infiltration without structural necrosis. In vitro replication kinetics comparing Madin-Darby canine kidney cells and primary chick embryo fibroblasts revealed rapid viral propagation in both cell types, though avian fibroblasts supported significantly higher viral yields during early replication phases between 12 and 48 hours post-infection. These mammalian studies demonstrate that despite bearing several molecular adaptation markers, NL04 possesses limited mammalian replication competence and low intrinsic pathogenicity in mice. Global epidemiological analysis of 5,029 historical H6Ny avian influenza isolates cataloged from 1963 to 2025 provided broader context on subtype evolution, ecological reservoirs, and interspecies transmission events. Annual isolation numbers remained low through the late 1990s before expanding sharply into an ongoing elevated circulation plateau that peaked in 2014 with approximately 440 recorded isolates. Avian species constituted the predominant ecological niche, representing 91.7 percent of the total dataset, whereas environmental samples accounted for 6.4 percent, and mammalian and human isolations remained exceptionally scarce at four and two cases respectively. Among the nine recognized neuraminidase pairings, H6N2 and H6N6 emerged as the dominant subtypes globally, accounting for 30.98 percent and 28.08 percent of all isolates, followed by H6N1 at 17.24 percent. While wild aquatic waterfowl constitute the natural ancestral reservoir, the surveillance dataset documented 145 separate spillover events of H6N2 into domestic chickens, demonstrating that terrestrial poultry now represent an established secondary niche that facilitates frequent encounters and reassortment with endemic poultry viruses. Given the commercial risks posed by circulating reassortants, the researchers formulated and tested a whole-inactivated oil-emulsion vaccine candidate derived from the homologous NL04 strain. Purified allantoic virus was inactivated using 0.1 percent beta-propiolactone for 24 hours at four degrees Celsius, followed by beta-propiolactone hydrolysis at thirty-seven degrees Celsius and high-speed emulsification with Montanide ISA 78 VG adjuvant. Three-week-old specific-pathogen-free chickens received a single intramuscular injection of 0.3 milliliters of the prepared vaccine formulation, with control animals receiving adjuvant-emulsified saline. Serum hemagglutination inhibition titers monitored weekly reached a mean of 13.25 log2 by five weeks post-immunization, confirming the induction of a potent humoral immune response. Following homologous live virus challenge at five weeks, vaccinated birds exhibited a substantially shortened and restricted shedding profile, shedding virus exclusively through the oral route and clearing infection within four days, whereas unvaccinated control birds shed virus predominantly through the oral route across the first week post-challenge. Neutralization assays conducted in embryonated chicken eggs further demonstrated that post-vaccination antisera effectively neutralized the homologous NL04 virus and exhibited cross-protective neutralizing activity against a heterologous H6N1 isolate collected from poultry in December 2025. In both cases, incubation of serum-virus mixtures abolished detectable hemagglutinating activity in harvested allantoic fluid, confirming that vaccine-induced antibodies recognize shared antigenic epitopes across distinct H6 lineages. The investigators identified key study limitations, including the lack of quantitative in vitro receptor-binding assays, the absence of aerosol droplet transmission experiments, the reliance on a single field isolate, and the need for vaccine efficacy validation under commercial field conditions with secondary pathogens. Nonetheless, the investigation underscores that the continuous genetic exchange between waterfowl-origin surface glycoproteins and the robust H9N2 G57 internal ribonucleoprotein machinery produces resilient reassortants capable of threatening commercial poultry health, highlighting the urgent need for sustained poultry genomic surveillance. Time
The outbreak was reported in December 2025, with the news article published on November 23, 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in Shangqiu, Henan Province, China, affecting a commercial layer flock of fifty-four-week-old chickens.
Event
A novel reassortant avian influenza virus, designated A/chicken/Henan/NL04/2025, was identified and characterized in a poultry outbreak in central China. This virus belongs to the low-pathogenic H6N2 subtype but carries an unusual genetic composition combining segments from different avian influenza lineages.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak caused a fifteen percent cumulative mortality rate in the affected flock. The virus was confirmed as low-pathogenic, lacking the consecutive basic amino acids typical of highly pathogenic strains. However, it exhibited virulence markers in the neuraminidase and other proteins that could potentially enhance its pathogenicity in mammalian hosts.
Response, status and uncertainty
The investigation involved whole-genome sequencing, postmortem examinations, and experimental inoculation of specific-pathogen-free chickens. The findings suggest the virus did not acquire internal segments through genetic drift but inherited an intact ribonucleoprotein cassette. However, the study had limitations, including the absence of in vitro receptor-binding assays and aerosol transmission experiments.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Uptick in H5N1 avian flu activity continues across North America - CIDRAP

News Brief
CIDRAP reported on November 25, 2026, that there is an uptick in H5N1 avian flu activity across North America. The report highlights ongoing surveillance and monitoring efforts but does not provide specific case counts, death rates, or detailed response measures. The focus is on the continued presence of the virus in avian populations, with no mention of human cases or public health interventions. The analysis is based on a news summary, and the full original article body was available for review. The report does not specify the scale of impact or risk level associated with the H5N1 activity, emphasizing the need for further investigation and monitoring.

中国报告一例来自越南的人感染H5N1禽流感病例

China reports human case of H5N1 bird flu in traveler from Vietnam
新闻简报据世界卫生组织 (WHO) 称,中国报告了一名来自越南的旅客感染 H5N1 禽流感病例。该男子居住在广宁省,于2026年11月4日出现症状,并在中国广西东兴口岸接受筛查。在港口采集的样本检测结果呈 H5N1 阳性,而后来在越南的检测结果呈阴性。该男子目前情况稳定,没有发烧、咳嗽或呼吸困难。他的感染源尚不清楚,卫生官员没有发现已知的家禽接触史或受污染的环境。尚未报告与此案件相关的其他案件。世界卫生组织西太平洋地区在 2026 年记录了 5 例人类 H5N1 病例和 1 例死亡,自 2020 年以来已出现 48 例病例和 18 例死亡。该病例凸显了该地区持续存在的 H5N1 禽流感风险,但没有提供有关应对或遏制措施的进一步细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
中国向世界卫生组织报告了该病例,该男子于 11 月 12 日出现症状。
地点与对象
该案件涉及一名来自越南(特别是广宁省)的旅客,事件发生在中国广西地区的东兴口岸。
事件
中国报告了一名来自越南的旅客感染 H5N1 禽流感病例。经东兴口岸检测确诊,该男子病情稳定,无发烧、咳嗽、呼吸困难等症状。
规模、影响与风险
尚未报告与此案相关的其他案件。世界卫生组织西太平洋地区2026年迄今已记录5例人类H5N1病例和1例死亡,自2020年以来已记录48例病例和18例死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
感染源尚不清楚。卫生官员没有发现已知的家禽、其他可能受感染的动物或受污染的环境的接触情况。该病例已报告给世界卫生组织,但没有提供有关应对措施的进一步细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

China reports human case of H5N1 bird flu in traveler from Vietnam

News Brief
China has reported a human case of H5N1 bird flu in a traveler from Vietnam, according to the World Health Organization (WHO). The man, who resides in Quang Ninh province, developed symptoms on November 4, 2026, and was screened at Dongxing Port in Guangxi, China. A sample taken at the port tested positive for H5N1, while later tests in Vietnam were negative. The man was in stable condition with no fever, cough, or difficulty breathing. The source of his infection remains unclear, and health officials found no known exposure to poultry or contaminated environments. No additional cases have been reported in connection with this case. The WHO’s Western Pacific region has recorded five human H5N1 cases and one death in 2026, with 48 cases and 18 deaths since 2020. This case highlights the ongoing risk of H5N1 bird flu in the region, though no further details on the response or containment measures are provided.
Original source text (English)

Murphy) Advertisment17 Share Tweet China has reported a human case of H5N1 bird flu in a man who lives in Vietnam and regularly crosses the border for business, according to the World Health Organization (WHO). The source of his infection is unclear. The man, who lives in Quang Ninh province in northern Vietnam, developed symptoms on Nov. 1. He had a cough, nasal congestion and a runny nose when he was screened the next day at Dongxing Port, a border crossing in China’s Guangxi region. A sample taken at the crossing tested positive for H5N1 in China, while later samples collected in Vietnam tested negative. By Nov. 4, the man was in stable condition with no fever, cough or difficulty breathing. China reported the case to the WHO between Nov. 13 and 19. Health officials found no known exposure to poultry, other potentially infected animals or contaminated environments. Article continues below the player Two people in the man’s household in Vietnam had no symptoms and tested negative for influenza A, including the H5 subtype. No additional cases have been reported in connection with the case. The WHO’s Western Pacific region has recorded five human H5N1 cases and one death so far in 2026. Since 2020, the region has recorded 48 cases and 18 deaths. That includes 23 cases and eight deaths from 2020 through 2024 and 20 cases and nine deaths in 2025. BNO News learned of the WHO report through Avian Flu Diary. 波兰报告六个月内首例禽流感 暴发 - The Poultry Site

Poland reports first bird flu outbreak in six months - The Poultry Site
新闻简报据家禽网站报道,波兰报告了六个月来首例禽流感 暴发。暴发 被识别为 H5N1,发生在一个火鸡场,该场 18,723 只火鸡中有​​ 587 只死亡,其余鸡群被扑杀。该报告于 2026 年 11 月 24 日发布,来源为联合摘要,这意味着完整的原始文章正文未经审查。暴发 强调了 禽流感 对家禽种群的持续风险,但没有提供有关检测、隔离或接触者追踪的具体细节。Poultry Site 是家禽业中享有盛誉的来源,该报告强调需要持续保持警惕并采取生物安全措施,以防止 H5N1 的传播。该事件是欧洲和其他地区 禽流感 监测和响应工作的更广泛背景的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 24 日(发布日期),证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
波兰,家禽养殖场,特别提到了火鸡养殖场。
事件
波兰报告了禽流感 暴发,被鉴定为 H5N1,18,723 只禽类中有 587 只死亡,其余被扑杀。该报告由家禽网站发布。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 报告有 587 只鸟死亡,其余鸟群(18,723 只鸟)被扑杀。暴发 与 H5N1 相关,H5N1 是 高致病性禽流感 亚型。没有提及人类感染或具体风险水平。
应对、状态与不确定性
报告了暴发,并采取了扑杀措施。来源是联合摘要,因此未审查完整的原始文章正文。回应包括遏制行动,但没有提供有关测试、隔离或接触者追踪的进一步细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Poland reports first bird flu outbreak in six months - The Poultry Site

News Brief
Poland has reported its first bird flu outbreak in six months, according to The Poultry Site. The outbreak, identified as H5N1, occurred on a turkey farm where 587 out of 18,723 birds died, and the remaining flock was culled. The report was published on 24 November 2026, and the source is a syndicated summary, meaning the full original article body was not reviewed. The outbreak highlights the ongoing risks of avian influenza in poultry populations, but no specific details on testing, isolation, or contact tracing were provided. The Poultry Site is a reputable source in the poultry industry, and the report underscores the need for continued vigilance and biosecurity measures to prevent the spread of H5N1. This event is part of a broader context of avian influenza monitoring and response efforts across Europe and other regions.
Original source text (English)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

世界卫生组织确认美国出现三例人类 H5N1 禽流感病例,其中一例死亡 - Inshorts

WHO confirms three human H5N1 bird flu cases, one death in US - Inshorts
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 于 2026 年 11 月 25 日在美国华盛顿确认了三例人感染 H5N1 禽流感病例,其中一人死亡。这三人都与奶牛场有关,那里的牛检测出病毒呈阳性。世界卫生组织表示,对公众的风险仍然较低,而职业暴露的风险为低至中度。Inshorts报道了这一事件,详细信息以世界卫生组织确认的信息为准。证据中没有提供有关调查或应对措施的进一步细节。这些病例被确定为确诊病例,风险评估基于世卫组织的官方声明。
查看新闻五要素
时间
世界卫生组织 (WHO) 于 2026 年 11 月 25 日在美国确认三例人类 H5N1 禽流感病例,其中 1 例死亡。事件日期为 2026 年 11 月 25 日。发布日期为 2026 年 11 月 25 日。
地点与对象
这些病例在美国华盛顿得到确诊。这三人都在奶牛场工作,那里的牛病毒检测呈阳性。
事件
世界卫生组织在美国华盛顿确认三例人感染 H5N1 禽流感病例,其中 1 例死亡。该事件已得到世界卫生组织(WHO)的确认。
规模、影响与风险
世卫组织坚持认为,公众面临的风险仍然较低,而职业暴露则构成低至中度风险。规模影响包括三例人类病例和一例死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
世界卫生组织确认了这些病例,并明确说明了风险评估。调查和风险评估正在进行中,未提供更多细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WHO confirms three human H5N1 bird flu cases, one death in US - Inshorts

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) confirmed three human cases of H5N1 bird flu in Washington, US, including one death, on 25 November 2026. All three individuals were associated with dairy farms where cattle tested positive for the virus. The WHO stated that the risk to the general public remains low, while occupational exposure poses a low to moderate risk. The event was reported by Inshorts, and the details are based on the confirmed information from the WHO. No further details on the investigation or response measures were provided in the evidence. The cases were identified as confirmed, and the risk assessment is based on WHO's official statement.

WHO maintains that the risk to the general public remains low, while occupational exposure poses a low to moderate risk. The scale impact includes three human cases and one death.
Response, status and uncertainty
The WHO confirmed the cases, and the risk assessment is explicitly stated. The investigation and risk assessment are ongoing, with no further details provided.

禽流感警报——爱尔兰中部地区两只野生天鹅 H5N1 检测呈阳性。- 瑟勒斯信息

Bird Flu Alert – Two Wild Swans Test Positive For H5N1 In Irish Midlands. - Thurles Information
新闻简报2026年11月21日,爱尔兰卫生和农业部门报告称,奥法利郡两只死亡野生天鹅的高致病性禽流感(HPAI)(也称为H5N1)检测呈阳性。此次检测是 监测 持续努力的一部分,突显了家禽业面临的日益严重的威胁。作为回应,针对家禽和圈养鸟类的强制性住房令将于 2026 年 11 月 23 日生效,要求鸟主防止与 野生鸟类 接触。农业部长马丁·海登(Martin Heydon)呼吁家禽饲养者(包括小型后院鸡群所有者)加强生物安全措施。公共卫生官员建议公众避免接触生病或死亡的 野生鸟类,并通过农业部的 Avian Check 应用程序报告疑似病例。虽然公众感染 H5N1 的风险被认为很低,但仍对情况进行积极监测,以保护 野生鸟类 人群和家禽业。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 11 月 21 日。事件日期没有明确说明,但检测发生在正在进行的 监测 期间。家禽和圈养鸟类的强制住房令将于2026年11月23日生效。
地点与对象
爱尔兰中部地区奥法利郡。受影响的人群包括爱尔兰的家禽和圈养鸟类饲养者,重点是该地区的 野生鸟类 人群。
事件
两只死去的野生天鹅的 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)(也称为 H5N1)检测呈阳性。卫生和农业部门对此进行了报告,作为正在进行的 监测 的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
H5N1 病毒得到证实,表明爱尔兰家禽业面临重大威胁。卫生当局认为公众感染的风险较低。
应对、状态与不确定性
家禽和圈养鸟类的强制性住房令将于 2026 年 11 月 23 日开始执行。敦促家禽饲养者加强生物安全措施。公共卫生建议包括不要接触病人或死者 野生鸟类 以及通过 Avian Check 应用程序报告疑似病例。目前正在积极监测和应对这一情况。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu Alert – Two Wild Swans Test Positive For H5N1 In Irish Midlands. - Thurles Information

News Brief
On 2026-11-21, health and agricultural authorities in Ireland reported that two dead wild swans in County Offaly tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI), also known as H5N1. This detection was part of ongoing surveillance efforts, highlighting the growing threat to the poultry industry. In response, a mandatory housing order for poultry and captive birds will take effect on 2026-11-23, requiring bird owners to prevent contact with wild birds. Minister for Agriculture Martin Heydon has called for increased biosecurity measures among poultry keepers, including small backyard flock owners. Public health officials advise the general public to avoid touching sick or dead wild birds and to report suspected cases via the Department of Agriculture’s Avian Check app. While the risk of H5N1 infection to the public is deemed low, the situation remains under active monitoring to protect both wild bird populations and the poultry sector.
Original source text (English)

Million International Cyber Fraud Investigation. » Bird Flu Alert – Two Wild Swans Test Positive For H5N1 In Irish Midlands. Health and agricultural authorities have issued a renewed warning after two dead wild swans in County Offaly tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI), commonly known as bird flu. The H5N1 virus was confirmed following ongoing surveillance of wild birds, highlighting the increasing threat to Ireland’s poultry industry. In response to the growing risk, a mandatory housing order for poultry and captive birds will take effect across Ireland on Monday, 23rd November 2026, requiring bird owners to prevent contact with wild birds. Minister for Agriculture Mr Martin Heydon has urged all poultry keepers, including small backyard flock owners, to strengthen biosecurity measures and remain alert for signs of infection. Public Health Advice: Members of the public are advised not to touch sick or dead wild birds and to report suspected cases through the Department of Agriculture’s Avian Check app. Health authorities currently consider the risk of H5N1 infection to the general public to be low. The latest detections serve as an important reminder of the need for continued vigilance to protect Ireland’s wild birds and poultry sector. 世卫组织报告美国乳制品工人出现 3 例 H5N1 感染,1 例死亡 - Arise News

WHO Reports Three H5N1 Infections In US Dairy Workers, One Death - Arise News
新闻简报世界卫生组织(WHO)报告美国华盛顿州亚基马县乳制品工人确诊三例H5N1感染病例,其中一例死亡。这些感染与接触受感染的牛或受污染的环境有关,并且没有观察到人与人之间的 传播。世界卫生组织指出,普通人群面临的风险仍然较低,但职业接触受感染动物的人的风险为低至中度。对暴露者和密切接触者的调查和监测正在进行中。该机构强调需要继续采取 监测 和公共卫生措施,以防止进一步接触。这些病例增加了自 2024 年以来美国报告的 75 例 H5N1 感染病例,凸显了在农业环境中监测人畜共患流感 暴发 的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
世界卫生组织(WHO)在2026年11月24日的更新中报告了这些病例。第一名患者于10月初出现症状,第二名患者于11月1日死亡。第三名患者出现结膜炎,但没有具体说明具体日期。
地点与对象
感染发生在美国华盛顿州亚基马县的奶牛场工人中。所有三名患者都是接触受感染奶牛的成年人。
事件
世界卫生组织确认美国乳制品工人中出现三例经实验室确诊的 H5N1 感染,其中 1 人死亡。这些感染与接触受感染的牛或受污染的环境有关,并且没有证据表明 传播 存在人际传播。
规模、影响与风险
报告了 3 例 H5N1 感染确诊病例,其中 1 例死亡。世界卫生组织表示,一般人群面临的风险较低,但职业接触受感染动物的人的风险为低至中度。未观察到人与人之间的 传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织正在继续监测 暴发、进行调查并追踪患者的接触者。正在敦促采取公共卫生措施来限制进一步的接触。目前情况仍在调查中,随着收集更多信息,风险评估可能会发生变化。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WHO Reports Three H5N1 Infections In US Dairy Workers, One Death - Arise News

News Brief
The World Health Organisation (WHO) reported three confirmed cases of H5N1 infections among dairy workers in Yakima County, Washington State, United States, with one death. The infections were linked to exposure to infected cattle or contaminated environments, and no human-to-human transmission was observed. The WHO noted that the risk to the general population remains low, but the risk for those with occupational exposure to infected animals is low to moderate. Investigations and monitoring of exposed individuals and close contacts are ongoing. The agency emphasized the need for continued surveillance and public health measures to prevent further exposure. These cases add to the 75 H5N1 infections reported in the US since 2024, highlighting the importance of monitoring zoonotic influenza outbreaks in agricultural settings.
Original source text (English)
ON NOW Arise 360 13:02, 25th Nov, 2026 by AriseNews WHO Reports Three H5N1 Infections In US Dairy Workers, One Death Global H5N1 infections occurred among farm workers exposed to infected cattle, while the global health agency says the risk to the general population remains low. Follow ARISE News on GoogleAdd us as a preferred source to see more of our stories in Google Follow us on Google The World Health Organisation (WHO) has reported three human infections involving the H5N1 strain of avian influenza in Washington State, United States, including one death among workers exposed to infected dairy cattle. In a disease outbreak update published on Tuesday, November 24, the organisation said the United States had notified it of three laboratory-confirmed infections identified between October 16 and November 4. All three patients were adults who worked on dairy cattle farms in Yakima County, where the highly pathogenic avian influenza virus had been detected among cattle. The first patient developed symptoms including eye irritation, a runny nose, muscle aches and headaches in early October. The individual sought medical attention and was not hospitalised. The second patient developed respiratory symptoms and later required hospital treatment for severe illness. The patient died on November 1, making the case the first reported death following exposure to infected dairy cattle or a related contaminated environment. Related News: Colorado Confirms Four Cases Of H5N1 Bird Flu Philippines Confirms Two New Mpox Cases, Raising Active Infections To Three UK Tops 50,000 Coronavirus Deaths as Italy Reaches One Million Infections The third patient developed conjunctivitis, an inflammation of the eye, but did not require hospitalisation. Laboratory testing and subsequent genetic analysis confirmed that the infections involved the H5N1 virus. The WHO said the viruses were highly similar to those detected in dairy cattle in Washington State, supporting the assessment that the infections resulted from exposure to infected animals or contaminated environments. The organisation said investigations and monitoring of people exposed to the affected cattle, as well as close contacts of the patients, were ongoing. However, it stressed that there was no evidence of human-to-human transmission associated with the three cases. Based on available information, the WHO assessed the risk posed by H5N1 to the general population as low. It said the risk was considered low to moderate for people with occupational exposure to infected animals. The latest cases add to the number of human infections reported in the United States since the country began documenting the outbreak. According to the WHO, 75 human infections involving influenza A(H5) viruses had been reported in the country since 2024, including the three cases covered in its latest update. Avian influenza viruses primarily circulate among birds and other animals but can occasionally infect humans following direct exposure to infected animals or contaminated environments. The WHO continues to monitor the outbreak and has called for surveillance, laboratory testing and public health measures to limit further exposure. The organisation said the situation could change as investigations produce additional information. It also urged continued monitoring of people working with potentially infected animals, particularly in areas where outbreaks have been detected. The agency’s assessment indicates that the latest infections require public health attention, but there is currently no evidence that the virus is spreading between people. Catherine Okonkwo. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

世界卫生组织 DON:甲型流感 (H5N1) - 美利坚合众国 - 禽流感日记

WHO DON: Influenza A(H5N1) - United States of America - Avian Flu Diary
新闻简报The Avian Flu Diary reports on a study from the University of Tokyo, published on November 25, 2026, which evaluated the protective effects of seasonal influenza vaccines against a human-derived bovine H5N1 virus in ferrets. The study found that live attenuated influenza vaccines (LAIV) induced stronger T-cell responses and provided substantial protection against lethal infection, while inactivated vaccines only reduced viral replication and delayed disease progression. In Washington State, nearly 6 weeks prior, the first dairy-cattle associated human infection with H5N1 was reported in Yakima County, followed by two more cases, one of which was fatal. The virus has caused outbreaks in 44 herds since early October 2024. The study involved influenza-naïve female ferrets, with 5 out of 6 surviving after LAIV vaccination and 0 surviving after inactivated vaccine. Close contacts of confirmed cases are being monitored, but details on the number and type of contacts are not available. The findings suggest that immune responses beyond serum antibodies, including cellular and potentially mucosal immunity, may contribute to cross-protection against antigenically distinct bovine-derived H5N1 viruses. However, these results are based on animal experiments and do not directly indicate effects in humans.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年11月25日、2026年11月26日、2024年初。
地点与对象
美利坚合众国,华盛顿州,奶牛,人类,雪貂。
事件
东京大学发表的一项研究评估了季节性流感 疫苗 对雪貂中人源性牛 H5N1 流感病毒的保护作用。研究发现,减毒活流感疫苗 (LAIV)可诱导更强的T细胞反应,并提供针对致命感染的实质性保护,而灭活的疫苗仅减少病毒复制并延缓疾病进展。
规模、影响与风险
Two human cases of H5N1 infection were reported in Washington State, with one fatality. The virus has caused outbreaks in 44 infected herds since early October 2024. The study involved influenza-naïve female ferrets, with 5 out of 6 surviving after LAIV vaccination and 0 surviving after inactivated vaccine.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是在雪貂身上进行的,并不直接表明对人类的感染预防或严重程度预防作用。华盛顿州公共卫生实验室使用 CDC 甲型流感 (H5) 检测对样本进行了检测。正在监测确诊病例的密切接触者,但尚不清楚接触者的数量和类型的详细信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WHO DON: Influenza A(H5N1) - United States of America - Avian Flu Diary

News Brief
The Avian Flu Diary reports on a study from the University of Tokyo, published on November 25, 2026, which evaluated the protective effects of seasonal influenza vaccines against a human-derived bovine H5N1 virus in ferrets. The study found that live attenuated influenza vaccines (LAIV) induced stronger T-cell responses and provided substantial protection against lethal infection, while inactivated vaccines only reduced viral replication and delayed disease progression. In Washington State, nearly 6 weeks prior, the first dairy-cattle associated human infection with H5N1 was reported in Yakima County, followed by two more cases, one of which was fatal. The virus has caused outbreaks in 44 herds since early October 2024. The study involved influenza-naïve female ferrets, with 5 out of 6 surviving after LAIV vaccination and 0 surviving after inactivated vaccine. Close contacts of confirmed cases are being monitored, but details on the number and type of contacts are not available. The findings suggest that immune responses beyond serum antibodies, including cellular and potentially mucosal immunity, may contribute to cross-protection against antigenically distinct bovine-derived H5N1 viruses. However, these results are based on animal experiments and do not directly indicate effects in humans.
Original source text (English)
Avian Flu Diary Covering Pandemic and Seasonal Flu, Emerging Infectious Diseases, public health, community & Individual preparedness. NOTE: All AFD blogs are written by a human - any mistakes are solely mine. Thursday, November 26, 2026 Nature Comms: Protective effect of seasonal vaccines against human-derived bovine H5N1 influenza virus (translated) Credit University of Tokyo Press Release #19,430 Given the enormous logistics of creating, testing, manufacturing, and deploying a pandemic strain-specific vaccine (see Maggie Fox's SCI AM - A Bird Flu Vaccine Might Come Too Late to Save Us from H5N1), many researchers have looked for potential stop-gap benefits from seasonal flu vaccination. It's not a new idea (see 2008's Seasonal Flu Vaccine May Offer Some Protection To H5N1 and 2010's Seasonal influenza vaccine elicits heterosubtypic immunity against H5N1 that can be further boosted by H5N1 vaccination), but more recent studies have provided mixed and modest results at best. Some of those studies include: Nature Comms: Adjuvanted Influenza Vaccination Increases Pre-existing H5N1 Cross-reactive Antibodies mBio: Low levels of influenza H5N1 HA and NA antibodies in the human population are boosted by seasonal H1N1 infection but not by H3N2 infection or influenza vaccination EID Journal: Effect of Seasonal Influenza Vaccines on Avian Influenza A(H5N1) Clade 2.3.4.4b Virus Infection in Ferrets EID Journal: Investigation of Influenza A(H5N1) Virus Neutralization by Quadrivalent Seasonal Vaccines, United Kingdom, 2021–2024 Our most recent foray into this topic was last September's EM&I: Limited added benefit of seasonal influenza vaccination before A(H5) vaccination in mice and ferrets challenged with A(H5N1), which offered little encouragement on the effectiveness of using seasonal flu vaccines as a `prime-boost' for H5N1 vaccines. That said, there are known, or suspected, benefits to having been recently vaccinated against seasonal flu in the face of a pandemic strain. It still may provide some small boost to your immune response. HA neutralizing antibodies aren't the only immune defense against infection. The N1 component from H1N1 may provide some degree of protection against severe illness, although unlikely to prevent infection It could help prevent a co-infection with H5N1 and seasonal flu, which has the potential for being more severe. Co-infections could also help generate a reassorted `hybrid' virus (see Preprint: Intelligent Prediction & Biological Validation of the High Reassortment Potential of Avian H5N1 and Human H3N2 Influenza Viruses). Today, we've a study out of the University of Tokyo led by Yoshihiro Kawaoka and Professor Ryuta Uraki, that introduces a new wrinkle. Instead of only testing the standard quadrivalent flu shot (inactivated influenza vaccine), it adds a trivalent live-attenuated influenza vaccine (LAIV; FluMist) to the mix. Flumist is currently only available for those aged 2-49 in the United States, and carries a number of exclusions, including pregnant women, immunocompromised individuals, or those who have recently received antiviral medications. As the graphic at the top of this blog indicates, those lab animals who received the live-attenuated influenza vaccine fared far better (83% survival vs 0%) than those which were inoculated with the standard flu shot. From the press release: When the immune responses involved in these protective effects were examined, no neutralizing antibodies against bovine-derived H5N1 highly pathogenic avian influenza virus were detected with any of the vaccines. On the other hand, the nasal attenuated live vaccine induced a stronger T-cell response to the virus than the inactivated vaccine. These results suggest that the nasal attenuated live vaccine also shows protective effects against bovine-derived H5N1 highly pathogenic avian influenza virus, and that this effect may be related to an immune response involving T cells. However, these results are based on animal experiments using ferrets and do not directly indicate infection prevention or severity prevention effects in humans. While an intriguing finding, there are a lot of caveats to consider, including: This was a small study using one H5N1 strain and a limited number of flu-naive ferrets. While useful in flu research, ferrets are not a perfect analog for humans, which invariably have a lengthy and messy history of exposures to influenza A viruses and/or vaccines. LAIV vaccines can only be safely used on a subset of society LAIV vaccines contain live-attenuated seasonal flu viruses, which could provide opportunities for a co-infection with a pandemic strain, with a theoretical potential for reassortment. The primary takeaway is not that LAIVs are a panacea for a pandemic, rather that nasal vaccines and T-cell immunity may be a promising direction for future pandemic flu protection. Of course, additional research might pave the way for LAIVs limited use in a future pandemic crisis. That said, it is a fascinating study, which many will want to read in its entirety. Protective effect of seasonal vaccines against human-derived bovine H5N1 influenza virus Published: 25 November 2026 Volume 17, article number 11796 (2026) Ryuta Uraki, Kiyoko Iwatsuki-Horimoto, Maki Kiso, Mutsumi Ito, Seiya Yamayoshi, Masaki Imai & Yoshihiro Kawaoka Abstract Since early 2024, highly pathogenic avian influenza H5N1 virus (clade 2.3.4.4b) has caused outbreaks in U.S. dairy cattle, infecting other mammals including humans. Here, we evaluate the protective efficacy of two commercially available seasonal influenza vaccines against a human-derived bovine H5N1 isolate, A/Texas/37/2024, in influenza-naïve female ferrets. All unvaccinated ferrets develop severe disease and succumb to infection or meet humane euthanasia criteria. Immunisation with an inactivated influenza vaccine reduces viral titres in multiple respiratory and extrapulmonary tissues and delays disease progression but does not prevent fatal outcomes. By contrast, five of six ferrets immunised with a live attenuated influenza vaccine survive challenge and show more effective control of viral replication in the nasal mucosa. Live attenuated vaccination induces stronger T-cell responses, while the absence of detectable neutralising activity against the challenge virus suggests that cellular immunity, rather than neutralising antibodies, may contribute to protection. These findings demonstrate that seasonal live attenuated influenza vaccination can provide cross-protection against lethal infection with an antigenically distinct bovine-derived H5N1 virus, potentially through cellular immune responses in the respiratory tract. (SNIP) Overall, seasonal live attenuated influenza vaccination provided substantial protection against lethal hu-TX37-H5N1 infection in influenza-naive ferrets, whereas the inactivated vaccine reduced viral replication and delayed disease progression but did not prevent mortality. These findings suggest that immune responses beyond serum antibodies, including cellular and potentially mucosal immunity, may contribute to cross-protection against antigenically distinct bovine-derived H5N1 viruses. (Continue . . . ) Wednesday, November 25, 2026 WHO DON: Influenza A(H5N1) - United States of America #19,429 Nearly 6 weeks ago Washington State reported their first dairy-cattle associated human infection with H5N1 in Yakima county. Since then 2 more cases have been reported (1 fatal) as the number of infected herds has grown to 44 since early October. Six days ago neighboring Oregon reported their first human case ( see Oregon: OHA Confirm's States 1st Dairy Associated Human HPAI Infection). Yesterday the World Health Organization published a DON (Disease Outbreak News) report on the first 3 dairy-linked cases from Washington. While a lengthy narrative, details on some of these cases are lacking; including exact dates of symptom onset, the dates H5 confirmation, and even whether or not they all worked on the same farm. Although this DON reports that `Close contacts of confirmed cases are also being monitored.', it also states that `Additional information on the number and type of contacts being followed was not available at the time of publication.' Unfortunately, the amount of detail we used to see in these types of reports has declined in recent years. Due to its length I've only posted some excerpts. Follow the link to read it in its entirety. Influenza A(H5N1) - United States of America 24 November 2026 Situation at a glance Between 16 October and 4 November 2026, the United States of America, through its International Health Regulations (IHR) National Focal Point, notified WHO of three laboratory-confirmed human infections with influenza A(H5) virus in Washington State. These included one fatal case, the first reported death following exposure to infected dairy cattle or a related contaminated environment. All three cases were adults who worked on dairy cattle farms in Yakima County, Washington State, where the highly pathogenic (in animals) avian influenza (HPAI) A(H5N1) virus had recently been detected in dairy cattle. Investigations and monitoring of exposed people and close contacts are ongoing. Based on information available to date, there is no evidence of human-to-human transmission. In accordance with the IHR (2005), any human infection caused by a new influenza A virus subtype is considered a potentially significant public health event and is subject to mandatory notification to WHO. Based on the information currently available on this and previous cases, WHO assesses the risk to the general population posed by A(H5N1) viruses as low. For individuals with occupational exposure to these viruses, the risk of infection is considered low to moderate. The situation may change as more information becomes available. WHO continues to closely monitor these viruses and the global epidemiological situation. Description of the situation On 16 October 2026, WHO was notified by the United States of America's International Health Regulations' National Focal Point of a laboratory-confirmed human infection with influenza A(H5) virus in Washington State. On 4 November 2026, two additional laboratory-confirmed human infections in the same state, including one fatal case, were notified to WHO. All three cases were adults aged 18 years or older who worked in Yakima County, Washington State, a major agricultural and dairy-producing area, where HPAI A(H5N1) virus had recently been detected in dairy cattle. It is not known if the cases worked on the same farm. The first case developed symptoms on 5 October 2026, including eye irritation, runny nose, muscle aches and headache, and sought medical care on 12 October. The case was not hospitalized and was offered treatment with oseltamivir. Eye (conjunctival) and nose and throat (nasopharyngeal) specimens were collected on 12 October and tested at the Washington State Public Health Laboratory using the United States Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) influenza A(H5) assay on 14 October. The conjunctival specimen tested positive for influenza A(H5) virus. Additional testing conducted by CDC determined this was an influenza A(H5N1) virus. The second case, who had underlying medical conditions, developed respiratory symptoms during epidemiological week 42, ending on 24 October 2026. The case presented to an emergency department the following week, was hospitalized with severe illness and died on 1 November. A respiratory specimen collected at the healthcare facility tested positive for influenza A virus and yielded a presumptive positive result for influenza A(H5) at the University of Washington. The Washington State Public Health Laboratory subsequently confirmed influenza A(H5) virus infection using the CDC influenza A(H5) assay. Further testing at the University of Washington identified the virus as influenza A(H5N1). The third case developed conjunctivitis and sought medical care but did not require hospitalization. During the week ending 31 October 2026, specimens collected from the case tested positive for influenza A(H5) virus at the Washington State Public Health Laboratory using the CDC influenza A(H5) assay. A specimen was subsequently sent to CDC for additional testing. Sequencing of the viruses from all three cases further confirmed the influenza A(H5N1) virus subtype. The sequences were highly similar to those of viruses detected in dairy cattle in Washington State. On 4 November 2026, the United States Department of Agriculture (USDA) reported that whole-genome sequencing of influenza A(H5N1) viruses detected in the first two affected dairy herds in Washington State identified genotype B3.13. According to the USDA, genetic analysis indicated that the detections were consistent with introduction of the virus from Idaho State, likely through cattle movement. Public health investigations, surveillance and monitoring of people exposed to infected animals or contaminated environments are ongoing. Close contacts of confirmed cases are also being monitored. Additional information on the number and type of contacts being followed was not available at the time of publication. The fatal case is the first death reported globally following exposure to infected dairy cattle. As of 20 November 2026, a total of 75 human cases of influenza A(H5) virus infection, including the three cases described in this report, had been reported in the United States of America since 2024. (Continue . . . ) Mexico: León Zoo (Guanajuato, Mexico) Closed Due to H5N1 Park Closure Announcement on X #19,428 Overnight the newshounds at FluTrackers picked up numerous Spanish language reports of an H5N1 outbreak at the León Zoo (Parque Zoológico de León) in Guanajuato, MX which has forced the temporary closure of the zoo (effective 11/25). A little over a week ago, León Zoo reported the detection of HPAI in dead migratory birds recovered at a central lake on the premises (see Agriculture Ministry press release below). Agriculture Ministry addresses case of avian influenza in migratory birds at the León Zoo The affected specimens are not part of the park's collection; Senasica is implementing preventative measures at the park and rules out any risk to commercial farms. Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development | November 16, 2026 | Press Release The WOAH WAHIS report - posted 2 days ago - lists 7 Swan Goose (WILD) confirmed with H5N1. In the past 12 hours, it has emerged that several zoo animals have since succumbed to (either suspected or confirmed) HPAI, including a primate and an ocelot. Some media reports indicate as many as 29 exposed individuals, with some (n=15) reportedly being `symptomatic'. Test results are pending, but as this is cold and flu season, these symptomatic cases could be unrelated. Although the Mexican media is filled with reports, official statements on government websites have yet to appear. For now, the best round-up of media reports can be found on this FluTrackers Thread. While this incident has the potential to become a much bigger story, early reports are often exaggerated, and so we'll need to wait for more details. Some past HPAI outbreaks in zoos include: Kentucky: Louisville Zoo Closes Some Bird Exhibits After Stork Dies From Avian Flu Canada: Calgary Zoo Closes Some Venues Over Bird Flu Concerns - Butterfield Acres Update Virginia: Richmond Zoo Reports Bird Deaths From HPAI H5 Chicago: Lincoln Park Zoo Confirms H5N1 Deaths of Harbor Seal & Flamingo Germany: Nuremberg Tiergarten (Zoo) Closed Over HPAI H5 Tuesday, November 24, 2026 One Health: Prevalence of asymptomatic influenza a virus infection in human and animal populations (Systematic Review & Meta-analysis) #19,427 Over the past 20 years we've revisited the topic of asymptomatic carriage and transmission of influenza A (in humans and other hosts) - and our generally myoptic surveillance and testing practices - many times, including: JAMA Open: Asymptomatic Human Infections With Avian Influenza A(H5N1) Virus Confirmed by Molecular and Serologic Testing (2025) The Air That We Breathe (Out) (2019) J.I.D.: Asymptomatic Summertime Shedding Of Respiratory Viruses (2018) EID Journal: Prevalence of Asymptomatic Influenza Virus Infections (2016) Asymptomatic Pigs: Revisited (2012) PLoS One: Influenza Viral Shedding & Asymptomatic Infections (2012) Pre-Symptomatic Transmission Of H1N1 Influenza In the Ferret Model (2012) EID Journal: Pre-Symptomatic Influenza Transmission (2011) EID Journal: Asymptomatic H5N1 In Pigs (2010) Every one of these studies presented evidence that asymptomatic or subclinical carriage of influenza A is not only common in humans (and other hosts), it can contribute significantly to the spread of these viruses. The notion that an infected individual must cough or sneeze in order to infect others has been challenged for years. In 2018's in PNAS: Infectious Virus Exhaled In Breath Of Symptomatic Seasonal Flu Cases, we looked at study that showed coughing and sneezing are not required to spread the flu virus. The authors wrote: “We found that flu cases contaminated the air around them with infectious virus just by breathing, without coughing or sneezing,” explained Dr. Donald Milton, M.D., MPH, professor of environmental health in the University of Maryland School of Public Health and lead researcher of this study. “People with flu generate infectious aerosols (tiny droplets that stay suspended in the air for a long time) even when they are not coughing, and especially during the first days of illness. So when someone is coming down with influenza, they should go home and not remain in the workplace and infect others.” Yet, we often see guidelines that prioritize testing symptomatic individuals who are exposed to avian or swine flu. The CDC's Recommendations for Monitoring and Testing of Individuals Exposed to A(H5N1) Viruses expressly states: Testing of asymptomatic pers...

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

北美 H5N1 禽流感活动持续上升

Uptick in H5N1 avian flu activity continues across North America
新闻简报文章报告称,北美地区 H5N1 禽流感活动有所增加,过去 30 天内在奶牛群中检测到 26 例,其中华盛顿州检测到 17 例。2026 年 10 月和 11 月,亚基马谷报告了三例 H5N1 人类病例,其中 1 例死亡与接触受感染的牛有关。在墨西哥,瓜纳华托州莱昂市动物园在死亡的迁徙水禽和两只圈养动物(一只蜘蛛猴和一只虎皮猴)身上确认感染 H5N1 暴发 后关闭。世界卫生组织指出,人类病例中没有人传人的 传播。美国农业部正在追踪该病毒的传播情况,但 暴发 的全面范围及其对人类健康的影响仍在调查中。关于麻疹 疫苗 豁免和 暴发 可能性的研究与所描述的 H5N1 活动无关。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年11月25日。人类 H5N1 病例的活动日期为 2026 年 10 月和 11 月,动物园 暴发 的活动日期为 2026 年 11 月 16 日。
地点与对象
暴发 在整个北美都有报道,特别是在华盛顿州和德克萨斯州的牛检测,以及墨西哥瓜纳华托莱昂市政动物园的动物园 暴发。受影响的人群包括奶牛群、圈养动物和奶牛场工作人员。
事件
北美观察到 H5N1 禽流感活动有所增加,奶牛群和人类病例中均检测到与接触受感染牛有关的病例。墨西哥暴发动物园导致一只蜘蛛猴和一只虎皮猴死亡,动物园关闭五天。
规模、影响与风险
过去 30 天,美国报告检测到 26 头牛,其中 17 头在华盛顿州。亚基马谷报告了三例人类 H5N1 病例,其中 1 人死亡。莱昂市动物园的 暴发 影响了多只动物,且 传播 人传人的风险尚未得到证实。
应对、状态与不确定性
世界卫生组织报告了人类病例,美国农业部正在监测牛的检测情况。暴发 过后,动物园已关闭五天。麻疹豁免和H5N1活动的研究与禽流感事件没有直接关系。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Uptick in H5N1 avian flu activity continues across North America

News Brief
The article reports an uptick in H5N1 avian flu activity across North America, with 26 detections in dairy cattle herds in the past 30 days, including 17 in Washington state. Three human cases of H5N1 were reported in Yakima Valley, October and November 2026, with one death linked to exposure to infected cattle. In Mexico, the Leon Municipal Zoo in Guanajuato closed after an H5N1 outbreak was confirmed in dead migratory waterfowl and two captive animals, a spider monkey and a tigrillo. The WHO noted no person-to-person transmission in the human cases. The US Department of Agriculture is tracking the spread, but the full extent of the outbreak and its impact on human health remain under investigation. The study on measles vaccine exemptions and outbreak likelihood is unrelated to the H5N1 activity described.

In the past 30 days, 26 cattle detections have been noted in dairy herds by the US Department of Agriculture’s Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS). Of the 26 detections, 17 have been in Washington state, with high activity in major dairy operations in the Yakima Valley. This week the World Health Organization detailed three human H5N1 cases reported in Yakima Valley in October and November, stating that all three patients were adults who worked on dairy cattle farms. No cases involved person-to-person transmission, and the report does not say if infected people all worked on the same farm. One of the case-patients (who had underlying health problems) died from the infection and was the first death reported globally following exposure to infected dairy cattle. The other two patients had mild illness, with conjunctivitis as an initial symptom. Zoo outbreak kills spider monkey, tigrillo In commercial poultry news, five new operations have reported H5N1 outbreaks this week, including a commercial broiler production in Sussex County, Delaware, affecting 269,900 birds. In the past 30 days, 4.02 million birds have been hit with avian flu in 32 US flocks, including 10 commercial and 22 backyard flocks, according to APHIS data. Finally in international news, Mexican health authorities announced the immediate closure of the Leon Municipal Zoo in the state of Guanajuato following an outbreak of H5N1. On November 16, the zoo noted the virus in dead migratory waterfowl found in the zoo’s central lake. Yesterday, testing showed H5N1 infection in two dead captive animals: a tigrillo (a small member of the cat family; see photo above) and a spider monkey, with a second spider monkey showing symptoms. The zoo is closed for the next five days. Nonmedical measles vaccine exemptions for kindergarteners tied to 16% higher risk of outbreaks News brief Mary Van Beusekom, MS Topics Measles Childhood Vaccines NIH Higher rates of nonmedical measles-vaccine exemptions for school entry were associated with a 16% higher outbreak likelihood in a study representing 237 million people in 30 US states. For the study, published yesterday in JAMA Network Open, a team led by a University of Washington researcher analyzed measles cases reported by the Johns Hopkins University Measles Tracker in 2025, state- and county-level data on measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine coverage among kindergarteners, and vaccine exemptions. The outbreak proxy was at least three measles cases per county, a count threshold rather than per-capita incidence; counties below that level were retained. The threshold didn’t establish epidemiologic linkage. The secondary outcome consisted of total county cases in counties with at least three cases rather than cases per outbreak. “Measles is among the most transmissible human pathogens, so even small, geographically clustered immunity gaps can sustain local transmission,” the authors wrote. “As aggregate national and state measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) coverage can obscure this local susceptibility, pockets of unvaccinated persons may persist even where aggregate coverage appears adequate.” Findings may guide school preparedness The analytic sample represented 71.6% of the US population, and 1,821 of 2,214 (82.2%) counties reported measles cases in 2025; 3.4% met the proxy. Average MMR coverage was 91.1%, and the average nonmedical exemption rate was 4.6%. Routinely collected exemption data may complement local knowledge when prioritizing school-level review and preparedness. The joint model showed a link between greater exemption rates and outbreak occurrence (odds ratio [OR], 1.16) but no association for MMR uptake (OR, 1.01). In the exploratory burden model, the incidence rate ratio was 1.08. Higher exemption rates were tied to lower MMR coverage. Joint-model ORs were 1.12 to 1.17 using alternative thresholds. Burden estimates remained positive but were less consistent in sensitivity analyses, with wide confidence intervals that indicated imprecision. The relationship between school nonmedical exemption rates and a higher likelihood of measles outbreaks may reflect clustering of susceptibility, differential measurement error in uptake and exemption data, or both, the authors said. “Routinely collected exemption data may complement local knowledge when prioritizing school-level review and preparedness,” they concluded. Higher HPV viral loads tied to increased risk of cervical cancer News brief Meghan Holohan Topics Human Papillomavirus (HPV) ronnachaipark / iStock Most people will experience a human papillomavirus (HPV) infection in their lifetime. High-risk strains of HPV cause cervical cancer in women, the fourth most common cancer in women worldwide. A paper published this week in the journal Cancer finds that viral load contributes to why some people develop cervical cancer, and others don’t. “Two women with similar HPV viral loads at one visit may have very different outcomes depending on how those levels change over time,” said co-corresponding author Lina Zhang, MD, in a news release from Wiley, the publisher of the journal. “Our findings suggest that a rising or persistently high viral load may signal a greater likelihood of cervical lesions.” Findings could lead to better monitoring Zhang and her colleagues at Nanjing Medical University in China led the study, which tracked 624 women (53.3%) who had persistent HPV infection, 471 (40.3%) who cleared their HPV infection, and 75 (6.4%) with a reactivated infection. Women with lingering HPV infections had higher viral loads than those who cleared it or had HPV reactivation. Those with moderate increasing or persistently high viral loads were more likely to develop cervical cancer than those with low stable or moderate declining viral loads. In total, 11% of women with low stable viral loads, 18% of women with moderate declining trajectories, 74% with moderately increasing viral loads, and 90% with persistently high viral loads had precancerous cervical lesions. Two women with similar HPV viral loads at one visit may have very different outcomes depending on how those levels change over time. Lina Zhang, MD Considering viral load could help doctors understand a woman’s future risk and create better surveillance plans for them. “We hope these findings will contribute to more personalized follow-up strategies and ultimately improve cervical disease prevention,” said co–corresponding author Zhonghua Shi, MD, in the release. Point-of-care test for syphilis receives emergency use authorization News brief Chris Dall, MA Topics Sexually Transmitted Infections Diagnostics quantic69 / iStock The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has issued emergency use authorization for a point-of-care molecular test for active syphilis, and three other pathogens that cause sexually transmitted infections (STIs). The test, developed by Roche Diagnostics, can detect Treponema pallidum—the bacterium that causes syphilis—from a single lesion swab sample within 15 minutes. It can also detect mpox virus and herpes simplex virus types 1 and 2. The three STIs can cause similar symptoms but require different treatments. Officials with the National Institutes of Health (NIH), which helped speed the review of the test through its Rapid Acceleration of Diagnostics Tech Independent Test Assessment Program, say the point-of-care test can help healthcare providers identify active syphilis infection and initiate appropriate treatment more quickly. “Point-of-care testing leads to faster treatment in the same visit, thus reducing transmission and worsening illness,” Bruce Tromberg, PhD, director of NIH’s National Institute of Biomedical Imaging and Bioengineering, said in an agency press release. Rise in syphilis cases It’s the first point-of-care test for syphilis to receive FDA authorization. Current syphilis diagnosis relies on blood-based serologic tests. US syphilis cases have risen by 42% since 2020, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, with 190,242 cases reported in 2024. Cases of congenital syphilis—the transfer of the infection from the mother to the fetus—have risen by 82%. If left untreated, syphilis can cause serious heart and brain damage, blindness, deafness, and paralysis. Congenital syphilis can lead to miscarriage, infant death, and lifelong medical issues. NIH says the test will become available in the United States in the coming months. RSV shots safe for older adults, alone or in combo, analysis finds News brief Liz Szabo, MA Topics Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Adult (non-flu) Vaccines lucigerma / iStock Multiple clinical trials have found that respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccines are safe in adults. Now, a new study in people age 60 and older shows it’s also safe to receive an RSV shot plus another vaccine in the same week. In a study of 18,252 people published this week in Vaccine, less than 1% of participants reported seeking medical attention during the week after being vaccinated against RSV. The study was based on reports to V-safe, a voluntary registry run by the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) that allows people to report side effects or other problems after receiving a vaccine. About one-third of people reported a mild or moderate health issue within a week after getting an RSV shot, including a sore arm, fatigue, muscle ache, and headache. About one in three people vaccinated against RSV received another vaccine within a week, according to the study, led by CDC scientists. Side effects differ by vaccine manufacturer People given the RSV vaccine made by Pfizer (Abrysvo) plus another shot were more likely to report side effects around the injection site compared with people who received the Pfizer shot alone. Study participants who received GSK’s RSV vaccine (Arexvy), either alone or with another vaccine, were more likely to report symptoms than those who received the Pfizer vaccine. Those who received the Moderna RSV vaccine (mResvia) had a similar risk of side effects as those given the Pfizer shot. The CDC recommends that people age 75 and older receive a single RSV shot. People older than 60 may also get an RSV vaccine if they are at high risk of developing severe disease from the illness. CWD digest: Fatal disease found on deer farms in Michigan, Kentucky News brief Mary Van Beusekom, MS Topics Chronic Wasting Disease Larry Smith / Flickr cc Michigan: The Michigan Department of Agriculture and Rural Development (MDARD) has confirmed the first-time detection of chronic wasting disease (CWD) in a cervid on a Roscommon County farm. While the type of cervid wasn’t identified in the news release, the farm raises game animals such as deer, elk, and reindeer, per media reports. Roscommon County, in the north-central part of Michigan’s Lower Peninsula, borders CWD-positive Ogemaw and Gladwin counties. “The presence of CWD in farmed cervid facilities is not new to Michigan; prior to this detection, cases have been found across multiple counties, including Calhoun, Hillsdale, Kent, Lake, Mackinac, Mecosta, Montcalm, Newaygo, and Osceola counties,” MDARD said. CWD is transmitted through infectious misfolded proteins called prions, which spread from animal to animal or through environmental contamination. Kentucky: A Shelby County deer farm is under quarantine after a deer tested positive for CWD last week—the first case in the county, the Kentucky Department of Agriculture reports. Shelby County is in the north-central part of the state and doesn’t border any CWD-positive county. “The Office of State Veterinarian established a management (5 mile radius) and surveillance (15 mile radius) zone for herds near the affected deer farm” and “is diligently reviewing recent movement records, and all deer farms in the management and surveillance zones are under restricted movement until further order of the State Veterinarian,” the news release said. 禽流感 暴发 传播至哺乳动物后莱昂动物园暂时关闭

León Zoo temporarily closes after bird flu outbreak spreads to mammals
新闻简报在 禽流感 暴发 病毒传播到哺乳动物后,墨西哥瓜纳华托莱昂动物园于 2026 年 11 月 25 日暂时向公众关闭。两只哺乳动物,一只蜘蛛猴和一只虎猴,病毒检测呈阳性并死亡,促使关闭。本月早些时候,动物园里还有三只鹅、两只野鸭和一只秃鹫死亡。官员怀疑候鸟传播了病毒,动物园园长强调,关闭是阻止疾病传播的预防措施。15 名工人在接触后出现了类似流感的症状,但没有人检测呈阳性。只有在相关部门评估并改善健康状况后,动物园才会重新开放。这一行动凸显了禽源人畜共患感染的风险,以及在野生动物饲养设施中管理此类 暴发 时保持警惕的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
莱昂动物园从 2026 年 11 月 25 日星期三开始暂时不对公众开放,预计关闭时间将持续 21 天。本月早些时候,据报道有三只鹅、两只野鸭和一只秃鹫死亡,并于 2026 年 11 月 26 日宣布关闭。
地点与对象
莱昂动物园位于墨西哥瓜纳华托州莱昂,受影响的种群包括鸟类和哺乳动物,特别是蜘蛛猴和虎猫(一种小型野猫)。
事件
在 禽流感 暴发 病毒传播给哺乳动物后,莱昂动物园暂时关闭,导致两只病毒检测呈阳性的哺乳动物死亡。关闭是一项预防措施,旨在阻止疾病传播。
规模、影响与风险
禽流感 已导致动物园内三只鹅、两只野鸭和一只秃鹫死亡。此外,有 15 名工人在接触带有 禽流感 的候鸟后出现了类似流感的症状,但没有人对该疾病检测呈阳性。关闭的目的是加强预防感染和保护公众健康的措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
作为预防措施,莱昂动物园已暂时关闭,并由专业人员继续为动物提供护理。动物园表示,是否重新开放将取决于健康状况和有关部门的评估。官员们认为,病毒可能是候鸟带来的,目前正在监测情况。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

León Zoo temporarily closes after bird flu outbreak spreads to mammals

News Brief
The León Zoo in Guanajuato, Mexico, temporarily closed its doors to the public on November 25, 2026, following an avian influenza outbreak that spread to mammals. Two mammals, a spider monkey and a tigrillo, tested positive for the virus and died, prompting the closure. Earlier this month, three geese, two wild ducks, and a vulture also died at the zoo. Officials suspect migratory birds introduced the virus, and the zoo’s director emphasized that the closure is a preventative measure to stop the spread of the disease. Fifteen workers developed flu-like symptoms after exposure, but none have tested positive. The zoo will reopen only after assessments by relevant authorities and improvements in the health situation. This action highlights the risk of avian-origin zoonotic infections and the need for vigilance in managing such outbreaks in facilities housing wild animals.

Home El Bajío El Bajío News León Zoo temporarily closes after bird flu outbreak spreads to mammals By MND Staff November 26, 2026 0 The León Zoo announced a 21-day closure after the deaths of two mammals that tested positive for avian influenza. (Sectur Guanajuato) The zoo in León, Guanajuato, temporarily closed its doors to the public this week amid an avian influenza outbreak that has killed birds and at least two mammals. Officials believe migratory birds may have introduced the virus to the zoo, located in the northeast of León, Guanajuato’s largest city. Zoo León announced its decision to close in a statement and a video message posted to social media. REFUERZA ZOOLEÓN MEDIDAS PREVENTIVAS ANTE PRESENCIA DE INFLUENZA AVIAR https://t.co/zKC7n8gmzK — Zooleon (@zooleon) November 25, 2026 “In accordance with the recommendations of both the federal Health Ministry and the state Health Ministry, we have taken the decision to temporarily close the León Zoo to the public starting Wednesday, Nov. 25,” the zoo’s director Diana Karen Casillas said on Tuesday. Casillas said that the decision to close the zoo is a “preventative measure” related to two cases of avian influenza (also known as bird flu) in mammals. Avian flu was confirmed in a spider monkey and a tigrillo (a small wild cat) that died at the zoo. The deaths of three geese, two wild ducks and a vulture at the zoo were reported earlier this month. Casillas said that the aim of the temporary closure — which is expected to last 21 days — is to “strengthen” measures aimed at stopping the spread of the disease. She said migratory birds brought avian flu to the zoo. “I want to stress that this situation is not exclusive to the León zoo,” Casillas said. “It can happen at any site that keeps wild animals,” she said. In its statement, Zoo León said that specialized zoo staff will continue feeding, monitoring and providing veterinary care to animals during the temporary closure. Casillas said in an interview that zoo personnel who work closely with animals use protective equipment. Fifteen workers developed flu-like symptoms after exposure to migratory birds with avian influenza, but to date none have tested positive for the disease, according to Guanajuato Health Minister Gabriel Cortéz Alcalá . The zoo thanked the public for its understanding during the temporary closure, and said that taking timely action is “essential to prevent infections, protect public health and safeguard animal life.” The zoo said its reopening will depend on how “the health situation” develops and on assessments by the relevant authorities. human-derived bovine H5N1 influenza virus 【字体: 大 中 小 】 时间:2026年11月26日 来源:Nature Communications 18.1 编辑推荐: 自2024年初以来,高致病性禽流感(Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza,HPAI)H5N1病毒(clade 2.3.4.4b分支)已在美国奶牛中引发疫情,并感染包括人类在内的其他哺乳动物。本研究评估了两种商用季节性流感疫苗对来源 欢迎领域“高通量小分子筛选解决方案”>> 为了促进科学的传播,生物通采集了这篇文章 订阅BioHot点这里解锁原文链接推送! “畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接 Article Open access 自2024年初以来,高致病性禽流感(Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza,HPAI)H5N1病毒(clade 2.3.4.4b分支)已在美国奶牛中引发疫情,并感染包括人类在内的其他哺乳动物。本研究评估了两种商用季节性流感疫苗对来源于人类的奶牛H5N1分离株A/Texas/37/2024在流感-na?ve(无免疫经验)雌性雪貂中的保护效力。所有未接种疫苗的雪貂均出现严重疾病,并因感染死亡或符合人道处死标准。接种灭活流感疫苗可降低多个呼吸道及肺外组织中的病毒滴度并延缓疾病进展,但无法防止致死性结局。相比之下,六只接种减毒活疫苗(Live Attenuated Influenza Vaccine,LAIV)的雪貂中有五只在攻毒后存活,并在鼻黏膜中表现出对病毒复制更有效的控制。减毒活疫苗接种诱导了更强的T细胞反应,而针对攻毒病毒未检测到中和活性,这提示保护性免疫可能依赖于细胞免疫而非中和抗体。这些研究结果表明,季节性减毒活流感疫苗接种可通过呼吸道细胞免疫应答,对一种抗原性不同的奶牛源H5N1病毒的致死性感染提供交叉保护。 自2024年初以来,高致病性禽流感(Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza,HPAI)H5N1病毒(clade 2.3.4.4b分支)在美国超过17个州的1,000余个奶牛群中持续流行,并发生由牛向包括人类在内的其他哺乳动物的传播事件,对公共卫生构成潜在威胁。研究人员此前报道,从人类病例中分离的奶牛源H5N1病毒A/Texas/37/2024可通过呼吸道飞沫在雪貂之间实现有效传播,凸显其跨种传播与持续致病的风险。当前常规使用的季节性流感疫苗所靶向的血凝素(Hemagglutinin,HA)抗原与流行奶牛H5N1病毒的H5 HA在抗原性上存在显著差异,但季节性疫苗诱导的神经氨酸酶(Neuraminidase,NA)交叉反应抗体以及针对核蛋白(Nucleoprotein,NP)等保守病毒蛋白的T细胞表位,可能提供一定程度的交叉保护。此外,经鼻内途径接种的减毒活疫苗(Live Attenuated Influenza Vaccine,LAIV)可诱导区别于肌肉注射灭活疫苗的黏膜免疫与细胞免疫应答。然而,上述交叉免疫反应能否有效抵御奶牛H5N1病毒的致死性感染,此前尚缺乏系统的实验证据。 瑞孚迪多肽药物研发解决方案>>领 取 为回答上述问题,研究人员在雪貂感染模型中评估了两种商用季节性流感疫苗——含Alhydrogel佐剂的四价灭活疫苗与三价减毒活疫苗FluMist——对人类来源奶牛H5N1分离株A/Texas/37/2024(hu-TX37-H5N1)的保护效力。研究团队通过监测临床疾病、存活时间、呼吸道与肺外器官病毒复制动态、组织病理学损伤、血清抗体应答谱及疫苗诱导的T细胞免疫水平,对两种疫苗平台的保护效果进行了多维度比较。该研究的结论对于H5N1禽流感大流行防范策略具有重要的现实意义:在特异性H5疫苗尚未广泛储备的情况下,现有季节性LAIV或可作为一种可快速动员的交叉保护手段。 研究人员采用的关键技术方法包括:基于已发表序列(EPI_ISL_19027114)利用反向遗传技术(reverse genetics)构建A/Texas/37/2024(H5N1)病毒;选用7至9月龄流感-na?ve雌性雪貂(经血清学确认对季节性流感A型和B型病毒阴性)为感染模型,实验方案获东京大学伦理委员会批准(批准号A2024PIA006-01);在生物安全三级(Biosafety Level 3,BSL-3)设施中完成活病毒攻毒;于第0天与第27天经肌肉注射进行两次免疫,间隔4周;采用血凝抑制试验(Hemagglutination Inhibition,HI)检测抗疫苗株抗体;采用聚焦减少中和试验(Focus Reduction Neutralization Test,FRNT)检测血清及鼻洗液中和活性;采用空斑试验(plaque assay)测定鼻拭子与器官病毒滴度;采用酶联免疫吸附试验(Enzyme-linked Immunosorbent Assay,ELISA)检测HA、NA、NP结合抗体;采用神经氨酸酶抑制试验(Neuraminidase Inhibition Assay,NAI)评估NA抑制活性;采用酶联免疫斑点试验(Enzyme-linked Immunospot,ELISpot)检测外周血单个核细胞(Peripheral Blood Mononuclear Cells,PBMCs)中γ-干扰素(IFN-γ)分泌以评价T细胞应答。样本队列来源方面,四价灭活疫苗与三价减毒活疫苗(LAIV)购自Denka Seiken与Daiichi Sankyo,疫苗株由日本国立传染病研究所提供。 Seasonal influenza vaccination induces antibody responses against the vaccine strains(季节性流感疫苗接种诱导针对疫苗株的抗体应答):研究人员通过HI试验检测免疫后雪貂血清,结果显示接种灭活疫苗的雪貂针对三种疫苗株(A/Victoria/4897/2022(H1N1pdm)、A/California/122/2022(H3N2)及B/Austria/1359417/2021(B/Victoria系))的HI滴度总体高于接种LAIV的雪貂。 Live attenuated vaccination protects ferrets from lethal hu-TX37-H5N1 infection(减毒活疫苗接种保护雪貂抵抗致死性hu-TX37-H5N1感染):以100 PFU的hu-TX37-H5N1进行鼻内攻毒后,未接种组11只雪貂全部出现进行性体重下降及/或体温降低,并在8天内死亡或达到人道处死标准;接种灭活疫苗的雪貂同样发生严重疾病,全部在12天内死亡或被人道处死,但存活时间较未接种组明显延迟,表明灭活疫苗仅提供部分保护;而接种LAIV的6只雪貂中有5只存活攻毒,尽管部分动物出现短暂体重下降但大部分恢复,显示出LAIV在抗致死性感染方面优于灭活疫苗。 Live-attenuated vaccination limits viral replication in the nasal mucosa(减毒活疫苗接种限制鼻黏膜中的病毒复制):通过空斑试验检测鼻拭子样本,研究人员发现由于攻毒剂量较低(100 PFU),第1天未检测到病毒;第3天和第5天,两组疫苗接种组的病毒滴度均显著低于未接种组;攻毒后第7天,所有存活的灭活疫苗免疫雪貂中仍可检测到传染性病毒,而6只LAIV免疫雪貂中有4只未检测到病毒,且LAIV组病毒滴度显著低于灭活疫苗组,表明LAIV对鼻黏膜病毒复制的控制更为有效。 Inactivated vaccine and live attenuated vaccine reduce viral replication in organs(灭活疫苗和减毒活疫苗降低器官中的病毒复制):于攻毒后第3天采集每组4只雪貂的嗅球、脑、鼻甲、肺和气管组织进行病毒滴定,结果显示未接种组所有动物全部检测器官均检出病毒,两种疫苗均降低大多数器官的病毒滴度,但灭活疫苗组鼻甲的病毒滴度降低幅度有限;组织病理学检查显示未接种组肺部呈现肺泡炎,伴中性粒细胞和巨噬细胞浸润及浆液性渗出,严重者可见肺泡壁坏死,且坏死区域与流感病毒NP抗原检测阳性区域一致;灭活疫苗组病变严重程度个体差异较大;LAIV组总体肺泡病变更轻,病毒抗原检测多为阴性,提示LAIV对上呼吸道的病毒复制控制优于灭活疫苗。 Live attenuated vaccine induces a stronger cellular immune response than inactivated vaccine(减毒活疫苗诱导强于灭活疫苗的细胞免疫反应):研究人员未发现针对hu-TX37-H5N1的可检测中和抗体(血清及鼻洗液均为阴性);针对季节性疫苗株H1、H3及乙型HA的结合抗体滴度灭活疫苗组更高;针对H5N1的N1 NA和NP的交叉结合抗体也呈类似趋势;LAIV组血清可轻微但显著抑制N1蛋白活性,而灭活疫苗组无此活性;ELISpot检测显示LAIV诱导的针对H5N1 NP的T细胞反应(IFN-γ分泌)显著强于灭活疫苗。因此,保护性免疫可能与细胞免疫及可能的黏膜免疫相关,而非中和抗体。 研究人员在讨论中指出,两种疫苗均诱导针对疫苗株的免疫应答并降低病毒复制,但临床结局差异显著;保护差异无法用血清抗体解释,因灭活疫苗诱导了更高的HI滴度和结合抗体,而两组均未诱导针对hu-TX37-H5N1的中和抗体;LAIV的微弱NAI活性不足以单独解释其保护优势;LAIV诱导了更强的T细胞反应,识别NP等保守蛋白的T细胞可能交叉反应并与疾病缓解相关。研究亦存在局限:流感-na?ve雪貂两次接种不能完全模拟人类既有免疫;缺乏雪貂特异性试剂,无法充分评估呼吸道局部分泌型免疫应答;部分灭活疫苗组动物在攻毒后晚期死亡,死亡原因不明。 研究结论指出,季节性减毒活流感疫苗接种可为流感-na?ve雪貂提供针对致死性hu-TX37-H5N1感染的实质性保护,而灭活疫苗虽可降低病毒复制并延缓疾病进展,但无法预防死亡;保护性免疫可能依赖于血清抗体之外的细胞免疫及潜在黏膜免疫应答,为应对奶牛源H5N1病毒的交叉保护策略提供了实验依据。 打赏 展开全文 开通VIP 享海量文章 全站开放获取+解锁超过 200万篇新闻全文+每日更新
