B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

俄勒冈州和华盛顿州禽流感病例仍在继续 - 你的俄勒冈新闻

Bird flu cases continue in Oregon, Washington - Your Oregon News
新闻简报文章报道,2026 年 11 月中旬,俄勒冈州和华盛顿州继续出现禽流感病例,特别是 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)。11 月 4 日,俄勒冈州的一家商业蛋鸡设施确认出现 HPAI,导致 94,200 只禽类被安乐死。11 月 4 日,华盛顿的一家商业食用蛋工厂因该疾病损失了 39,600 只禽类。此外,HPAI 一直在奶牛群中传播,自 10 月以来,华盛顿州已有 44 头奶牛群受到影响。俄勒冈州于 11 月 19 日报告了首例人类 HPAI 病例,与莫罗县奶牛场最近发生的 暴发 病例有关。美国农业部将病毒的传播归因于野生水禽迁徙,野生水禽迁徙将病毒带回了秋季地区。该文章没有提供有关检测或接触者追踪等具体应对措施的详细信息,但强调了对太平洋西北地区高致病性禽流感病例的持续监测和报告。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年11月24日。俄勒冈州和华盛顿州于11月中旬报告了禽流感病例,具体日期包括11月17日、19日、20日和28日。西方最严重的病例发生在11月18日。
地点与对象
这些病例在俄勒冈州和华盛顿州报告,特别是在马里恩县、克拉索普县和皮尔斯县。受影响的种群包括后院鸡群和商业运营中的鸟类,以及华盛顿和俄勒冈州的奶牛群。
事件
11 月中旬,俄勒冈州和华盛顿州继续出现 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 病例,两起案件各有 40 只鸟被安乐死。此外,华盛顿州的奶牛群中也证实存在 HPAI,俄勒冈州于 11 月 19 日报告了首例人类 HPAI 病例,与一家奶牛场 暴发 有关。
规模、影响与风险
在俄勒冈州,11 月 4 日,HPAI 在一家商业产蛋设施中杀死了 94,200 只家禽。在华盛顿,11 月 4 日,一家商业食用蛋工厂失去了 39,600 只家禽。该疾病还影响了奶牛群,自 10 月以来,华盛顿州有 44 头奶牛群受到影响。华盛顿州于 11 月 19 日报告了一起人类病例,俄勒冈州于 11 月 24 日报告了另一起人类病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部确认俄勒冈州和华盛顿州出现禽流感病例,病毒的传播归因于野生水禽的迁徙。文章没有提及检测、隔离或接触者追踪等具体应对措施,但强调了对高致病性禽流感病例的持续监测和报告。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu cases continue in Oregon, Washington - Your Oregon News

News Brief
The article reports that bird flu cases, specifically highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI), continued in Oregon and Washington in mid-November 2026. In Oregon, HPAI was confirmed in a commercial egg layer facility on November 4, resulting in the euthanization of 94,200 birds. In Washington, a commercial table egg operation on November 4 lost 39,600 birds due to the disease. Additionally, HPAI has been spreading in dairy herds, with 44 herds affected in Washington since October. Oregon reported its first human case of HPAI on November 19, linked to a recent outbreak at a Morrow County dairy farm. The USDA attributes the spread of the virus to wild waterfowl migration, which brings the virus back to regions in the fall. The article does not provide details on specific responses like testing or contact tracing, but it emphasizes the ongoing monitoring and reporting of HPAI cases in the Pacific Northwest.
Original source text (English)

Highly pathogenic avian influenza tends to reemerge in the fall as migrating waterfowl, which carry the virus, return to regions. (USDA/Contributed) Getting your Trinity Audio player ready... California farm loses 89,000 birds in mid-November case Cases of highly pathogenic avian influenza continued to occur in non-commercial backyard flocks in Oregon and Washington in mid-November, according to USDA data. Oregon had bird flu cases Nov. 19 in Marion County and on Nov. 20 in Clatsop County. In each instance, 40 birds were euthanized. Washington had cases in Pierce County on Nov. 17, which killed 60 birds, and on Nov. 20, when 10 birds were affected. The most significant instance of HPAI in the West in mid-November came Nov. 18 from Fresno County, Calif., where 88,900 birds in a commercial broiler breeder operation were killed. In late October and early November, however, California and each state in the Pacific Northwest had major commercial cases. The disease began to reemerge at that time because wild waterfowl spread the virus as they migrate. An Oct. 28 case affected 1.3 million birds at a commercial egg layer in Stanislaus County, Calif. On Nov. 4, HPAI killed 94,200 birds at a commercial egg layer facility in Clackamas County, Oregon. Also on Nov. 4, a Pierce County, Wash., commercial table egg operation lost 39,600 birds due to the disease. On Nov. 5, HPAI killed 7,000 birds at a commercial game bird farm in Gooding County, Idaho, according to USDA data. HPAI also hitting dairy herds Bird flu also is spreading in dairy herds. Oregon reported its first human case of HPAI this year on Nov. 19, which was connected to a recent case at a Morrow County dairy farm. On Nov. 1, a Yakima County, Wash., resident exposed to infected cattle died from bird flu. On Nov. 23, USDA confirmed bird flu in dairy cattle in Washington’s Grant County. Washington has now had 44 dairy herds stricken by avian influenza since the first case was confirmed Oct. 8. The Morrow County outbreak is Oregon’s only case of HPAI in dairy herds in the past 30 days, as of Nov. 24. HPAI has been detected in 17 cases in dairy herds in Washington during the past month. However, there have been more than 40 such instances in Washington since early October. Idaho has had two detections of HPAI in dairy herds in the past month, while California has had none. Time
The article was published on November 24, 2026. Bird flu cases were reported in Oregon and Washington in mid-November, with specific dates including November 17, 19, 20, and 28. The most significant case in the West occurred on November 18.
Location and population
The cases were reported in Oregon and Washington, specifically in Marion County, Clatsop County, and Pierce County. The affected population includes birds in backyard flocks and commercial operations, as well as dairy herds in Washington and Oregon.
Event
Cases of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) continued in Oregon and Washington in mid-November, with 40 birds euthanized in each of two instances. Additionally, HPAI was confirmed in dairy herds in Washington, and Oregon reported its first human case of HPAI on November 19, linked to a dairy farm outbreak.
Scale, impact and risk
In Oregon, HPAI killed 94,200 birds at a commercial egg layer facility on November 4. In Washington, a commercial table egg operation lost 39,600 birds on November 4. The disease also affected dairy herds, with 44 herds impacted in Washington since October. A human case was reported in Washington on November 19, and another in Oregon on November 24.
Response, status and uncertainty
The USDA confirmed bird flu cases in Oregon and Washington, and the spread of the virus is attributed to wild waterfowl migration. The article does not mention specific responses like testing, isolation, or contact tracing, but it highlights the ongoing monitoring and reporting of HPAI cases.

格兰特县出现首例病例后,华盛顿州已有 44 头奶牛群受到 H5N1 感染 - Dairynews.today

Washington reaches 44 H5N1-affected dairy herds after first case in Grant County - Dairynews.today
新闻简报继 2026 年 11 月 23 日格兰特县出现首例确诊病例后,华盛顿州已有 44 个奶牛群受到 H5N1 禽流感 影响。这是该州第三个受影响的县,之前的 暴发 仅限于亚基马县和本顿县。基因测序和流行病学调查证实,暴发 与牛群流动有关,而不是 野生鸟类。当局已隔离受影响的农场,并强调巴氏杀菌牛奶和乳制品仍然安全。华盛顿州农业部 (WSDA) 和格兰特卫生区正在每月进行牛奶检测和设施检查,同时在受影响地区每周进行 监测。乳制品工人面临更高的风险,建议穿戴防护装备。虽然公众面临的风险较低,但 2026 年已报告了 3 例人类病例,其中 1 例因基础疾病死亡。该州目前在牛 H5N1 检测方面与德克萨斯州并列全国第四,仅次于加利福尼亚州、爱达荷州和科罗拉多州。
查看新闻五要素
时间
第一例病例于2026年11月23日确诊,自2026年10月8日以来受影响的牛群总数达到44头。发布日期为2026年11月24日。
地点与对象
华盛顿州,特别是格兰特县,奶牛群受到影响。该州此前已在亚基马县和本顿县记录了病例。
事件
华盛顿州于 2026 年 11 月 23 日确认在格兰特县的奶牛中首次检测到 禽流感。这标志着该州第三个受影响的县。暴发 与州和农场之间的牛运输相关,而不是 野生鸟类。
规模、影响与风险
自 2026 年 10 月以来,已有 44 个奶牛群受到影响,华盛顿州的牛 H5N1 检测量在全国排名第四。由于尚未发现人与人之间的 传播,因此公众面临的风险被认为较低。不过,乳制品工人面临的风险较高,建议采取防护措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
华盛顿州农业部 (WSDA) 和格兰特卫生区已实施生物安全协议、每月散装罐牛奶测试和设施检查。卫生当局已确认巴氏灭菌牛奶和乳制品是安全的,他们建议消费者和宠物主人避免使用未经巴氏灭菌的产品。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Washington reaches 44 H5N1-affected dairy herds after first case in Grant County - Dairynews.today

News Brief
Washington State has reached 44 dairy herds affected by H5N1 avian influenza, following the first confirmed case in Grant County on November 23, 2026. This marks the third affected county in the state, with previous outbreaks confined to Yakima and Benton counties. The outbreak is linked to cattle movements rather than wild birds, as confirmed by genetic sequencing and epidemiological investigations. Authorities have quarantined the affected farm and emphasized that pasteurized milk and dairy products remain safe. The Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA) and Grant Health District are conducting monthly milk testing and facility inspections, alongside weekly surveillance in affected areas. Dairy workers are at higher risk and are advised to wear protective gear. While the risk to the general public is low, three human cases have been reported in 2026, including one death with underlying medical conditions. The state is now tied with Texas for fourth place nationally in H5N1 detections in cattle, behind California, Idaho, and Colorado.

Interview Events DairyRally US DairyRally Azerbaijan Upcoming events Presentations Photoreports Other Milk Camel Sheep Goat Mare Buffalo About Us Washington reaches 44 H5N1-affected dairy herds after first case in Grant County Indonesia 24.11.2026 662 Authorities confirmed the first detection of avian influenza in dairy cattle in Grant County on November 23, 2026, making it the third affected county in Washington. The farm was quarantined; health authorities reiterated that commercially available pasteurized milk and dairy products are safe. The Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA) and the Grant Health District announced the detection, the first confirmed in the state since November 5. Washington recorded its first case on October 8, 2026, and previous outbreaks had been confined to Yakima and Benton counties. With 44 herds affected since October, the state is tied with Texas for fourth place nationally in detections of highly pathogenic avian influenza in cattle, behind California, Idaho and Colorado. Genetic sequencing and epidemiological investigations link the infections to cattle movements between states and farms, rather than introductions from wild birds. According to an analysis by the United States Department of Agriculture's Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS), the H5N1 genotype B3.13 detected in all three counties matches the virus introduced into Washington through cattle from Idaho. It subsequently spread among cows on different dairy farms. Veterinarians are treating sick animals at the Grant County farm, which is subject to strict biosecurity protocols. WSDA continues monthly bulk-tank milk testing and facility inspections statewide, alongside weekly surveillance in affected communities. State veterinarians urge prompt reporting of symptoms such as thicker milk ranging from yellowish to brown, sharp declines in milk production and feed intake, fever, respiratory problems, changes in manure and reduced rumination. Recommendations for producers include limiting movements of lactating cows between farms, isolating newly arrived animals for 30 days and avoiding sharing vehicles or equipment. They also advise separating cows suspected of infection, keeping abnormal milk out of bulk tanks and restricting cattle access to open water frequented by wild waterfowl. WSDA and the Grant Health District said pasteurization effectively inactivates H5N1 and that they do not anticipate milk shortages. Because infected cows shed the virus primarily through raw milk, health authorities advise consumers and pet owners to avoid unpasteurized dairy products. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and the Washington State Department of Health consider the risk to the general public low; no person-to-person transmission has been identified in the United States. Dairy workers face a higher risk of exposure and are advised to wear gloves, masks, eye protection and fluid-resistant coveralls. Washington has recorded three human cases among workers at Yakima dairy farms in 2026, including one death involving a person with underlying medical conditions. Over the past 30 days, the United States Department of Agriculture has confirmed infections in 26 dairy herds across seven states, including one case in neighboring Oregon. Since March 2024, the cumulative national total has reached 1,236 affected herds across 26 states. Time
The first case was confirmed on November 23, 2026, with the total number of affected herds reaching 44 since October 8, 2026. The publication date is November 24, 2026.
Location and population
Washington State, specifically Grant County, with dairy herds affected. The state has recorded cases in Yakima and Benton counties previously.
Event
Washington State confirmed the first detection of avian influenza in dairy cattle in Grant County on November 23, 2026. This marks the third affected county in the state. The outbreak is linked to cattle movements between states and farms, not wild birds.
Scale, impact and risk
44 dairy herds have been affected since October 2026, placing Washington fourth nationally in H5N1 detections in cattle. The risk to the general public is considered low, as no person-to-person transmission has been identified. However, dairy workers are at higher risk and are advised to take protective measures.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA) and Grant Health District have implemented biosecurity protocols, monthly bulk-tank milk testing, and facility inspections. Health authorities have confirmed that pasteurized milk and dairy products are safe, and they are advising consumers and pet owners to avoid unpasteurized products.

格兰特县奶牛中发现致病性禽流感;消费者警告不要食用生奶 - KOMO

Pathogenic bird flu found in Grant County dairy cattle; consumers warned against raw milk - KOMO
新闻简报据华盛顿州农业部 (WSDA) 称,华盛顿州格兰特县的一个奶牛场已确认 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)。该病毒被鉴定为 H5N1,通过常规 监测 检测到,并由美国农业部国家兽医服务实验室确认。暴发 已敦促采取隔离措施,并呼吁采取严格的生物安全措施,以防止农场之间进一步传播。WSDA 强调,巴氏灭菌奶制品对消费者来说仍然是安全的,而生奶和未经巴氏灭菌的乳制品由于可能存在病毒而被认为是不安全的。该机构还敦促奶农报告其牛群的疾病迹象,例如牛奶外观的变化、产量下降和呼吸道症状。卫生官员指出,目前 禽流感 对公众的风险较低,但与受感染动物密切接触的人应采取预防措施。正在调查 HPAI 在奶牛之间的传播,有证据表明 传播 通过受污染的材料和农场之间的人员流动传播。WSDA 强调了奶农在限制病毒传播方面所做的努力,并鼓励他们继续保持警惕和合作。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 23 日星期一。
地点与对象
美国华盛顿州格兰特县;奶牛群。
事件
高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 在格兰特县的一个奶牛群中得到确认,引发了隔离并呼吁采取严格的生物安全措施。华盛顿州农业部 (WSDA) 报告在牛群中检测到 H5N1,这一发现得到了美国农业部国家兽医服务实验室的证实。
规模、影响与风险
该病毒是通过例行程序 监测 检测到的,并得到了美国农业部的确认。高致病性禽流感可以通过受感染的动物和受污染的牛奶、设备、车辆、衣服、鞋类和其他材料或在农场之间移动的人员在奶牛之间传播。疾病控制与预防中心和华盛顿州卫生部认为 禽流感 对公众的风险较低。
应对、状态与不确定性
受影响的奶牛群正在接受检疫,并已实施严格的生物安全措施。兽医正在治疗生病的奶牛,WSDA 敦促农民报告疾病的临床症状。该机构还建议消费者避免使用生牛奶和生乳制品,并寻找经过巴氏灭菌或超巴氏灭菌的标签。病毒在奶牛群之间的传播正在接受调查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pathogenic bird flu found in Grant County dairy cattle; consumers warned against raw milk - KOMO

News Brief
Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) has been confirmed in a dairy herd in Grant County, Washington, according to the Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA). The virus, identified as H5N1, was detected through routine surveillance and confirmed by the USDA National Veterinary Services Laboratories. The outbreak has prompted a quarantine and calls for strict biosecurity measures to prevent further spread between farms. WSDA emphasized that pasteurized milk products remain safe for consumers, while raw milk and unpasteurized dairy products are considered unsafe due to the potential presence of the virus. The agency also urged dairy farmers to report signs of illness in their herds, such as changes in milk appearance, decreased production, and respiratory symptoms. Health officials noted that the risk to the public from avian influenza is currently low, but those with close contact with infected animals should take precautions. The spread of HPAI between dairy cattle is being investigated, with evidence suggesting transmission through contaminated materials and people moving between farms. WSDA highlighted the efforts of dairy farmers in limiting the virus's spread and encouraged continued vigilance and cooperation.

Why did H5N1 spread from Idaho to Washington dairies? topics: H5N1 Avian influenza Dairy cattle Quarantine Biosecurity Raw milk Pasteurization Washington GRANT COUNTY, Wash. — Highly pathogenic avian influenza has been confirmed in a Grant County dairy herd, prompting a quarantine and renewed calls for strict biosecurity as state and federal officials investigate how the virus is spreading between farms. The Washington State Department of Agriculture said on Nov. 23 that it identified highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) H5N1 in the herd. SEE ALSO | L&I inspects two Central Washington dairies after worker dies from bird flu The virus was detected by the Washington Animal Disease Diagnostic Laboratory through routine surveillance, and the finding was confirmed by the USDA National Veterinary Services Laboratories. USDA genetic sequencing analysis indicates the H5N1 strain currently circulating in Washington dairy herds is consistent with a virus introduced from Idaho through cattle movement. Powered By 10 Sec Seattleites turn to to-go Thanksgiving feasts, easing prep while boosting local economy Next Stay Investigations indicate the virus has spread from cow to cow between dairy herds. While wild birds can carry and spread HPAI, they are not believed to be the source of transmission between Washington dairy herds, WSDA said. The affected dairy herd is under quarantine and has employed strict biosecurity measures, according to WSDA. Veterinarians are working to treat sick cows. WSDA said pasteurized milk products remain safe for consumers and milk supply shortages are not expected. Pasteurization inactivates viruses and bacteria in milk, including HPAI virus, and consumers are urged to look for “pasteurized” or “ultra-pasteurized” on labels. WSDA warned that infected cows can shed the virus in their milk, making raw milk, cheese, and other raw unpasteurized dairy products unsafe for consumption. Health officials told consumers to avoid raw milk and raw dairy products, particularly those from infected or untested farms, because they may contain harmful viruses and bacteria. SEE ALSO | How concerned should you be about recent bird flu cases in Washington state? WSDA also advised people not to allow pets or other animals to consume raw milk or other dairy products, or raw foods such as raw meat or dead birds. WSDA urged dairy farmers statewide to report clinical signs of illness to their veterinarian, including changes in milk appearance (thicker, often yellowish to brown), decreased milk production, decline in feed intake, fever, respiratory symptoms, changes in manure, and a steep drop in rumination and rumen motility. The agency said affected animals should be isolated and farmers should contact their veterinarian and WSDA at 360-902-1878 or ahealth@agr.wa.gov. Most cattle affected by HPAI recover with supportive care, WSDA said, with full herd recovery typically taking four to six weeks. “I know another detection is discouraging, especially for the dairy farmers who have worked so hard to protect their workers and their herds. But I want to recognize what this community has accomplished. Dairy farmers across Eastern Washington have worked together, shared what they’ve learned and supported one another through an incredibly challenging time. The fact that we’ve been able to limit the virus’s spread across the region is a significant accomplishment and speaks to the dedication of our farmers, veterinarians and farmworkers,” said Washington State Veterinarian Amber Itle, VMD, MS. “I encourage everyone to keep going, stay the course and continue looking out for one another. The work you’re doing matters, and it is making a difference.” WSDA said HPAI can spread between dairy cattle through infected animals and contaminated milk, equipment, vehicles, clothing, footwear and other materials or people that move between farms. Wild birds can also carry HPAI, but they are considered a rare source of introduction and spread to dairy herds. Poultry are also highly susceptible to HPAI transmitted by any other affected species, and other states have reported infections in commercial and backyard poultry flocks near dairy farms affected by H5N1. Poultry owners, including those with backyard flocks, are urged to remain vigilant for signs of illness and practice strong biosecurity. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and the Washington State Department of Health currently consider the risk to the public from avian influenza to be low, WSDA said. Okeechobee egg farm tests positive for bird flu, will cull nearly 1.8M chickens - WPBF

Okeechobee egg farm tests positive for bird flu, will cull nearly 1.8M chickens - WPBF
新闻简报On November 22, 2026, the Okeechobee egg farm in Florida tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza, prompting the culling of nearly 1.8 million chickens. This action, taken by Cal-Maine Foods, the largest egg company in the U.S., includes approximately 1.4 million laying hens and 360,000 pullets. The Florida Department of Agriculture and Consumer Services established a quarantine zone covering Okeechobee and parts of St. Lucie County. The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) stated that the risk to the general public is low, though they monitor individuals with animal exposures. Since February 2024, the CDC has reported 75 human cases and three deaths linked to H5 bird flu in the U.S. The company emphasized collaboration with federal, state, and local authorities to mitigate future outbreaks and manage the response effectively.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于 2026 年 11 月 22 日报道,更新提到周四建立隔离区,根据发布日期,该日期为 2026 年 11 月 19 日。
地点与对象
The Okeechobee egg farm in Florida, specifically in Okeechobee and St. Lucie County, is the location. The affected population includes the approximately 1.8 million chickens at the farm, which is part of Cal-Maine Foods, the largest egg company in the U.S.
事件
The Okeechobee egg farm tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza, leading to the culling of nearly 1.8 million chickens. The company halted production, and the Florida Department of Agriculture established a quarantine zone.
规模、影响与风险
The culling affects approximately 1.4 million laying hens and 360,000 pullets. The CDC considers the risk to the general public as low but monitors people with animal exposures. There have been 75 human cases and three deaths of H5 bird flu in the U.S. since February 2024.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Okeechobee egg farm tests positive for bird flu, will cull nearly 1.8M chickens - WPBF

News Brief
On November 22, 2026, the Okeechobee egg farm in Florida tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza, prompting the culling of nearly 1.8 million chickens. This action, taken by Cal-Maine Foods, the largest egg company in the U.S., includes approximately 1.4 million laying hens and 360,000 pullets. The Florida Department of Agriculture and Consumer Services established a quarantine zone covering Okeechobee and parts of St. Lucie County. The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) stated that the risk to the general public is low, though they monitor individuals with animal exposures. Since February 2024, the CDC has reported 75 human cases and three deaths linked to H5 bird flu in the U.S. The company emphasized collaboration with federal, state, and local authorities to mitigate future outbreaks and manage the response effectively.

Advertisement Updated: 5:29 PM EST Nov 22, 2026 Editorial Standards i Allen Cone Assignment Editor Okeechobee egg farm tests positive for bird flu, will cull nearly 1.8M chickens Cal-Maine Foods halts production, state establishes quarantine zone Updated: 5:29 PM EST Nov 22, 2026 Editorial Standards i Allen Cone Assignment Editor OKEECHOBEE, Fla. — The largest egg company in the United States reported that its operation in Okeechobee County tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza, which will result in the ultimate depopulation of approximately 1.4 million laying hens and 360,000 pullets. Production has ceased at the plant, which follows the protocols prescribed by the U.S. Department of Agriculture on bird flu. The closure represents 2.7% of flock of the company, which is headquartered in Ridgeland, Mississippi. Advertisement "Cal-Maine Foods continues to work closely with federal, state and local government officials and focused industry groups to mitigate the risk of future outbreaks and effectively manage the response," the company said in a statement. The Florida Department of Agriculture and Consumer Services established a quarantine area that includes Okeechobee and stretches into St. Lucie County on Thursday. "Avian influenza is a serious disease concern for the poultry industry and animal health officials," the state agency said. "Influenza virus strains vary considerably in severity, and their pathogenicity, or ability to kill poultry, is labeled as either highly pathogenic with large mortality, or low pathogenic with little or no mortality." Florida Department of Agriculture Bird flu quarantine area. The agency regularly monitors domestic poultry for evidence of avian influenza viruses. Samples are regularly collected from backyard flocks, show birds, commercial poultry and live bird markets. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention c onsiders the risk to the general public to be low but the agency "is watching the situation carefully and working with states to monitor people with animal exposures." The CDC has reported 75 human cases of H5 bird flu and three deaths in the U.S. since February 2024: 45 involving dairy cattle, 24 poultry farms and three other animals. There are more than 378.5 million egg-laying chickens in the United States. In 2023, more than 9.4 billion broiler chickens and 218 million turkeys were processed in the United States. 波尔克县商业家禽群中确诊禽流感 - WEAU

Bird flu confirmed in commercial poultry flock in Polk County - WEAU
新闻简报2026年11月24日,威斯康星州农业、贸易和消费者保护部报告称,在威斯康星州波克县的一家商业养鸡场中发现了一例高致病性禽流感的确诊病例。受影响的场所已被隔离,以限制禽鸟及禽鸟产品的流动,并且该地产的所有鸟类已被清除,以防止疾病传播。该养鸡场的鸟类将不会进入食品系统。此次应对行动由威斯康星州农业、贸易和消费者保护部与美国农业部动物健康官员联合进行。目前该情况正在积极管理中,尚未报告具体病例数或死亡数。此事件凸显了禽流感和暴发在商业养鸡场中持续存在的风险,并展示了为遏制病毒传播所采取的措施。
查看新闻五要素

威斯康星州农业、贸易和消费者保护部于 2026 年 11 月 24 日证实了此案。

威斯康星州农业、贸易和消费者保护部报告了商业家禽群中出现的一例 高致病性禽流感 确诊病例。
规模、影响与风险
受影响的场所已被隔离,家禽和家禽产品已受到限制,该场所的鸟类数量已减少,羊群中的鸟类将不会进入食品系统。没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
威斯康星州农业、贸易和消费者保护部和美国农业部动物卫生官员正在联合应对事件。情况已确认并正在积极管理中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu confirmed in commercial poultry flock in Polk County - WEAU

News Brief
A confirmed case of highly pathogenic avian influenza was reported in a commercial poultry flock in Polk County, Wisconsin, on November 24, 2026, by the Wisconsin Department of Agriculture, Trade and Consumer Protection. The affected premises have been quarantined to restrict movement of poultry and poultry products, and all birds on the property have been depopulated to prevent the spread of the disease. Birds from the flock will not enter the food system. The response involves a joint effort between the Wisconsin Department of Agriculture, Trade and Consumer Protection and U.S. Department of Agriculture animal health officials. The situation is under active management with no specific case or death counts reported. This event highlights the ongoing risk of avian influenza outbreaks in commercial poultry operations and the measures taken to contain the spread of the virus.
Original source text (English)

世卫:美国报告3例感染禽流感病例 1人死亡

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

新华社日内瓦11月24日电 世界卫生组织24日说,美国向该组织通报了3例人感染甲型H5禽流感病毒病例,其中1人死亡。目前世卫组织评估该病毒对一般公众的风险较低。 根据世卫组织官网的消息,这3人均为成年人,在美国华盛顿州亚基马县一家奶牛场工作,该地区近期已在奶牛中检测到高致病性H5N1型禽流感病毒。迄今尚无证据表明存在人际传播。世卫组织将继续密切监测。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
